At the end of 2017, the growth rate of foreign
currency deposits fell to -0.1%.
Foreign
currency
term
deposits,
recalculated
in
Moldovan lei, decreased by 276.7 million lei or
by 1.7% per year, whereas sight deposits
increased by 263.0 million lei or by 3.3%.
At the end of 2017, the share of domestic
currency deposits in total deposits amounted
to 58.5%, whereas foreign currency deposits
accounted for 41.5%, having modiﬁed during
the reference year by 3.8 p.p.
in favour of
domestic currency deposits.
In the structure of domestic currency deposits,
the share of term deposits in total deposits
placed in Moldovan lei recorded 54.2%, having
decreased by 8.7 p.p. at the end of December
2017 compared to the same period of 2016.
Lending market
During the reporting year, the growth rate of
the total balance of loans in the economy,
extended by the licensed banks, recorded
negative annual dynamics. This evolution was
driven
by
the
negative
dynamics
of
the
balance of corporate loans, accounting for a
share of around 79.9%. At the end of 2017, the
balance of loans in the economy reduced its
negative growth rate, compared to December
2016 (-3.4% compared to -7.4%). The balance
14Money supply M3 includes money supply M2 and
foreign currency deposits placed by residents, recalculated
in Moldovan lei.
42


of corporate loans recorded a negative growth
rate of 7.8%, which increased by 1.9 p.p.
compared to the end of 2016. This evolution
was driven by the decrease in both domestic
currency loans by 8.2% and foreign currency
loans, recalculated in domestic currency, by
7.4%. It is worth noting that the growth rate of
the balance of retail loans remained positive
throughout the year as a result of the revival
of both consumer and real estate loans (Chart
3.8).

In terms of domestic currency loans, in 2017,
the annual growth rate has mitigated the
decline, so that the total balance of loans
granted in the economy did not change at the
end of December 2017, year-on-year (Chart
3.9). The negative annual growth rate of the
total balance of loans granted in the economy
was driven by the evolution of foreign currency
loans, recalculated in Moldovan lei, as a result
of the appreciation of the domestic currency
against the main reference currencies.
It
should
be
noted
that
the
volume
of
dollar-denominated
foreign
currency
loans
continued
to
grow
and
recorded
positive
annual growth rates starting with October
2017 (Chart 3.9).
Evolution of new granted loans
In 2017, the annual volume of new granted
loans recorded an increase, amounting to
24 457.5 million lei, having risen by 8.9%,
compared to 2016. This evolution was driven
by domestic currency loans, the volume of
which grew by 19.7%,
totalling 15 389.6
million lei (Chart 3.10).

Domestic currency loans strengthened their
growth through the rise in both retail and
corporate loans.
The annual volume of
corporate domestic currency loans increased
by 15.2%, reaching 9 689.3 million lei and
accounting
for
63.0%
of
total
domestic
currency loans.
The volume of retail loans,
holding a share of 37.0% of total loans,
increased
by
28.0%,
compared
to
2016,
totalling 5 700.3 million lei.
43


In 2017, compared to 2016, the volume of
foreign currency loans, recalculated in lei,
decreased by 5.6%, being mainly driven by the
appreciation of the domestic currency against
the main reference currencies, and recorded
9 067.9 million lei (Chart 3.11).
The largest
share in total foreign currency loans was held
by corporate loans. In 2017, the total volume
of
corporate
foreign
currency
loans,
recalculated in lei, decreased by 5.1% and
recorded 9 003.1 million lei.
The developments recorded in loans sector
during 2017 reﬂect the effects of a steady
decrease in interest rates applied on new
domestic currency loans,
being driven by
monetary policy decisions implemented by the
National Bank of Moldova.
Deposit market
During 2017, the growth rate of the total
balance of deposits placed with the licensed
banks recorded positive annual dynamics. This
evolution was driven by the positive dynamics
of the balance of domestic currency deposits,
which accounted for over 58%. At the end of
2017, the year-on-year change in the balance
of deposits decreased slightly, compared to
December 2016 (8.9% vs. 9.8%) (Chart 3.12).
At the end of 2017, the balance of corporate
deposits recorded a growth rate of 23.4%,
having increased by 8.2 p.p., compared to the
end of 2016. It should be mentioned that the
growth rate of the balance of retail deposits
recorded positive values throughout the year
as a result of the evolution of sight deposits.

In terms of the domestic currency deposits,
in 2017, their annual growth rate has slowed
down, so that the total balance of deposits, at
the end of December 2017, recorded a growth
rate of 16.4%, by 6.4 p.p. lower than at the
end of December 2016. The overall evolution of
the balance of deposits was adversely affected
by the evolution of foreign currency deposits,
recalculated in Moldovan lei, which at the end
of 2017 recorded a negative annual rate of
0.1%. It should be mentioned that the volume
of US dollar-denominated deposits continued to
grow and recorded positive annual growth rates
since April 2017, reaching 16.7% per annum at
the end of December 2017.
Evolution of new attracted deposits
In 2017, the annual volume of new deposits
placed with banks has recorded a downward
trend, having grown by 38 997.6 million lei,
their volume decreasing by 17.6%, compared
to 2016. The decline was driven by a notable
decrease in the volume of new domestic
currency deposits, the total volume of which
declined by 23.0%, recording 24 755.7 million
lei (Chart 3.13).
The largest share in the
volume of new domestic currency deposits was
held by retail deposits.
In 2017, the total
volume of retail domestic currency deposits
decreased by 24.8%,
recording 18 092.8
million lei, corporate deposits decreased by
17.6% and totalled 6 662.9 million lei.

Interest rates applied on new deposits
and loans
During 2017,
the Executive Board of the
National Bank of Moldova carried out four
consecutive cuts of the base rate applied to
the
main
monetary
policy
operations.
Following a downward trend set by the NBM’s
base rate applied to the main monetary policy
operations, the monthly weighted average
interest rates on new deposits and loans
taken/extended
during
2017
have
also
recorded a decline (Chart 3.15).
Thus, the
weighted average interest rate on new granted
loans fell from 11.55% per annum, recorded in
January
2017,
to
9.58%
per
annum
in
December 2017 (minimum value) (Chart 3.15).

New loans
The weighted average interest rate on new
loans extended in domestic currency in 2017
recorded a level of 10.33% per annum, having
decreased by 3.88 p.p., compared to 2016.
This development was driven by the evolution
of interest rates on both corporate and retail
loans. The weighted average interest rate on
new corporate loans, extended during 2017,
recorded a level of 10.40%, having dropped by
3.67 p.p., compared to 2016. The weighted
average interest rate on retail loans, extended
in 2017, decreased by 4.26 p.p., falling to
10.20% per annum, compared to 2016.
A decline in the level of interest rates applied
on new foreign currency loans recorded a
slower dynamics.
The weighted average
interest rate on foreign currency loans dropped
from 5.26% per annum, recorded in January
2017, to 4.66% per annum in December 2017
(Chart 3.11). In 2017, the weighted average
rate on foreign currency loans recorded 5.01%
per annum, having decreased by 0.89 p.p.,
compared to 2016. The rate decline was driven
by the evolution in interest rates applied both
on new corporate loans, accounting for the
largest share of total loans, and new retail
loans, extended in foreign currency.
New deposits
Throughout 2017, following the downward
trend set by the NBM’s base rate applied to
the main monetary policy operations and as a
result
of
the
excess
liquidity
condition
recorded in the banking system, the interest
rates
applied
on
new
deposits
recorded
decreases both in case of domestic currency
deposits
and
foreign
currency
deposits.
Interest rates on domestic currency deposits
attracted by banking system have gradually
declined to 5.19% per annum (December
2017),
recording a decrease of 1.61 p.p.,
compared to January 2017 (Chart 3.13). The
weighted average rate of domestic currency
deposits placed in 2017 recorded a level of
5.73% per annum, by 4.98 p.p. lower than in
2016. This evolution was driven by the interest
rate applied on retail deposits. The weighted
average interest rate on retail deposits in 2017
recorded 6.07% per annum, having decreased
2.1-fold, compared to 2016.
The average interest rate on foreign currency
deposits recorded a downward trend, having
dropped by the end of the year to 1.37% per
annum (Chart 3.14).
The weighted average
interest rate on foreign currency deposits,
placed in 2017, recorded a level of 1.63% per
annum, by 0.49 p.p.
lower than in 2016.
Average weighted interest rates applied on
both corporate and retail deposits recorded
virtually identical levels. The average rate on
45


corporate deposits fell to 1.62% per annum,
recording a level by 0.31 p.p. lower than in
2016. The weighted average interest rate on
retail foreign currency deposits declined by
0.53 p.p.
Banking margin
In 2017, in the domestic currency segment,
following a notable decline in the interest rate
applied on deposits,
the banking margin,
expressed in MDL, increased by 1.11 p.p.,
compared to 2016, recording a level of 4.60%.
In the foreign currency segment, the situation
was opposite, the decline in the interest rate
applied on loans recording a faster dynamics
than in case of deposits; as a result, the
banking margin, expressed in foreign currency,
recorded
an
annual
decline
of
0.40
p.p.,
dropping to 3.38% (Chart 3.16).

Balance interest rates
During 2017, the average interest rates on the
balance of loans extended by licensed banks
continued
their
downward
trend,
having
recorded, at the end of 2017, an average level
of 9.83% per annum (Chart 3.17) for domestic
currency loans (-1.97 p.p.
compared to the
end of 2016). This decrease was driven by the
average interest rate on domestic currency
loans, largely as a result of the developments
recorded in the retail loans segment.
The
developments were attributed to a notable
decrease in loan costs,
which were also
recorded in the corporate segment, although
the average interest rate of the latter recorded
a smaller decline.
These developments reﬂected the effects of the
relaxation of the monetary policy, promoted
by the National Bank of Moldova in the last
eight quarters, as well as of the excess liquidity
accumulated in the banking system.
During 2017, the interest rates on foreign
currency
loans
balance
recorded
developments similar to those of domestic
currency loans, though recording a smaller
decline, reaching 5.15% per annum at the end
of 2017, thus having decreased by 0.59 p.p.,
compared to the end of 2016. This decrease
was also partly driven by the excess of foreign
currency
recorded
on
the
market
in
the
reporting period.
Following the downward trend set by the base
rate applied to the main monetary policy
operations of the National Bank of Moldova,
the weighted average interest rates on the
balance of deposits taken by the licensed
banks continued their decline.
Thus, at the end of 2017, the average interest
rate on the balance of domestic currency
deposits recorded 5.69% per annum, having
decreased by 2.83 p.p., compared to the end
of 2016 (Chart 3.17).
This evolution was
mainly driven by the decrease in the average
interest rates on the balance of domestic
currency
deposits
placed
by
individuals,
whereas the decline in the corporate segment
recorded lower dynamics.
At the end of 2017, the average interest rate
on the balance of foreign currency deposits
recorded a new historical low, reaching 1.34%
per annum in the reference period, having
dropped by 0.85 p.p., compared to the end of
the previous year.
The annual decline was
driven by a sharp fall in average interest rates
on the balance of retail deposits, whereas
interest rates on corporate deposits recorded a
negligible decline during the year.

3.5
Monetary market
Primary market of state securities
As a state agent responsible for the placement
and administration of state securities (SS)
through the Book-Entry System, the National
Bank, in 2017, organised and carried out 193
auctions for SS placement.
Taking into account the inﬂow of external
ﬁnancing, mainly for budget support, coming
from the European Commission, the World
Bank, the Romanian Government and the IMF,
the Ministry of Finance showed consistency in
its SS issuance policy, structure and volumes
of SS sales at the beginning of 2017 as well as
throughout most of the year. Thus, treasury
bills of 91-, 182-, 364-days maturity were
offered for sale in weekly volumes of 40.0,
60.0
and
70.0
million
lei,
respectively.
Government bonds (GB) with 2-year maturity
and ﬂoating interest rate were offered though
the
monthly auctions
during the
year in
volumes similar to those of the previous year –
15.0 million lei. The intention of the Ministry of
Finance to increase the average maturity of SS
as well as an increased investors’ interest for
SS with a longer maturity resulted in the
placement of bonds with 2-year maturity and
ﬁxed interest rates. They were put up for sale,
together
with
3-year
maturity
bonds,
in
monthly volumes of 20.0 million lei for each
maturity.
In the ﬁrst quarter of 2017, the Ministry of
Finance created a cash buffer by issuing two
government bonds with 1- and 2-year maturity.
This reserve was meant to ease seasonal
pressures in order to provide the necessary
ﬁnancing for the budget deﬁcit. Also, it was to
contribute to the decrease of the reﬁnancing
and
liquidity
risks,
thus
increasing
the
efﬁciency of government debt management.
The decrease in the interest rate on SS placed
on
the
primary
market
affected
the
attractiveness of these investments. The ratio
between the volume of bids submitted and the
offered supply, recorded throughout 2017,
decreased, having dropped to 1.5 (compared
with 1.7 in 2016).
The Ministry of Finance
managed to attract 10 293.0 million lei from
the
market,
slightly
below
the
projected
amount of 10 530.0 million lei (Chart 3.18).

Non-bank investors remained active on SS
market due to the attractiveness of SS interest
rates; however, volumes purchased by them
during 2017 (1 833.5 million lei) diminished,
both as an absolute value (-312.3 million lei)
and as a share in the total volume traded
(from 19.1% to 17.8%) as compared to 2016.
The balance of SS purchased by non-residents
amounted to 15.0 million lei at the end of
2017, having slightly increased compared to
the end of 2016.
47


These developments were accompanied by
positive changes applied to the structure of
the newly issued SS: the share of 91-day SS
has been signiﬁcantly decreased in favour of
GB with a 2- and 3-year maturity (Charts 3.20
and 3.21).
Under these conditions,
the
average maturity of issued SS recorded a
considerable increase (from 234 to 287 days).

In 2017, SS interest rates recorded values
located mainly at the lower part of the rate
corridor set by the NBM. The analysis of the
evolution of these rates in the maturity proﬁle
shows a reduced volatility, compared to 2016,
associated with a subunit difference invariably
recorded between them throughout the year
(Chart 3.22).
At the last SS auction held in
2017, the average SS nominal interest rate for
91-day maturity recorded a similar level of
5.16% as at the end of the previous year.
Interest rates for SS with 182- and 364-day
maturity reached 5.93% and 5.98%, having
increased
by
0.34
p.p.
and
0.51
p.p.,
respectively,
compared
to
interest
rates
applied on SS with similar maturity, issued at
the end of 2016.

market, SS interest rates, during the ﬁrst two
months of 2017, were lower than interest rates
applied on deposits taken by banks. Given the
low investors’ interest in these investments,
SS interest rates recorded a leap in March,
positioning themselves and hence oscillating
within the corridor formed by interest rates
on loans extended and deposits placed in the
economy, through the end of the year. The gap
between the interest rates on loans extended
and deposits taken, speciﬁc for 2016, remained
unchanged almost throughout the entire 2017
(Chart 3.23).

Secondary market of state securities
In the reporting year, the turnover of the
secondary market of SS recorded 43.3 million
lei (in 2016 – 30.2 million lei), up 43.4%
compared to the previous year, the bank-client
transactions accounting for the highest share
(62.9%) in total SS transactions, as in the
previous years.
Over
2017,
a
slight
modiﬁcation
in
the
weighted average interest rate was recorded
on the secondary market of SS, as opposed to
the
previous
year,
when
a
reverse
development took place. Thus, in January, the
weighted average rate recorded 5.63%, having
risen by the end of the year in December to
5.65% (Chart 3.25).

Keeping record of securities through the
Book-Entry System (BES) of the NBM
The Book-Entry System (BES) of securities is a
system destined for the storage and settlement
of state securities and certiﬁcates issued by the
NBM.
The system ensures the processing of:
• transfer orders, as well as the ﬁnal and
irrevocable
settlement
of
securities
transactions in this system;

• operations related to the issuer (primary

• operations on the secondary market (sale-

At the end of 2017, 14 participants were
registered in the BES, including 11 banks, the
National Bank of Moldova, the Ministry of
Finance, and the Deposit Guarantee Fund of
the banking system.
As
of
31
December
2017,
BES
records
reﬂected securities in the total amount of

could be classiﬁed, by the issuer, as follows:

of 22 929.3 million lei, including:
50


• 7 544.8 million lei or 32.9% representing
SS issued on the primary market through
auctions;
• 2 093.3 million lei or 9.1% representing
SS transferred to the National Bank as a
result of the conversion of loans, initially
contracted
from
the
NBM
and

• 13 291.2 million lei or 58.0% representing

235 of 03.10.2016 on the
government
bonds
issued
for
the
execution by the Ministry of Finance of
the payment obligations derived from
state guarantees no. 807 of November
17, 2014 and no. 101 of 1 April 2015.
II. The National Bank of Moldova – the NBM
Certiﬁcates (NBC) issued through auctions for
a total amount of 9 230.2 million lei.
Total amount of SS, recorded in the BES at
nominal value, is classiﬁed by holders, as
follows:
Banks:
• As of 31.12.2016 – 5 341.4 million lei
• As of 31.12.2017 – 6 191.4 million lei
Non-bank investors (including the Deposit
Guarantee Fund of the banking system):
• As of 31.12.2016 – 1 463.5 million lei
• As of 31.12.2017 – 1 353.4 million lei
The National Bank of Moldova:
• As of 31.12.2016 – 15 446.5 million lei
• As of 31.12.2017 – 15 384.5 million lei
Thus, as of 31 December 2017, the share held
by the National Bank is still higher than that of
other investors (Charts 3.30 and 3.31).
The NBM certiﬁcates in the amount of 5 821.3
million lei were recorded in commercial bank
portfolios and 104.7 million lei in the portfolios
of other investors.

transactions settled in the BES amounted to

The CHIBOR yield curve recorded a signiﬁcant
decline in January, having returned, after a
long period of time, within the limits of the
NBM rate corridor. Over the following months,
the CHIBOR yield curve was broadly stable.
However, the banks’ reaction to the monetary
policy decision adopted at the end of April
regarding the increase in the ratio of required
reserves
held
in
Moldovan
Lei
and
in
non-convertible currency was a ﬁrm one, the
CHIBOR interest rates recording an increase.
Following the decrease in the NBM base rate at
the end of June, interest rates assumed a
slightly downward trend, which have followed
through the end of the year. Overall, reference
rates have fallen by about 4 p.p. compared to
the end of the previous year.
At the end of 2017, the CHIBOR 2W15 interest
rate recorded 9.45% compared to 13.28% at
the end of 2016.
15Reference rate calculated based on the quotes of
contributing banks for 2-week term deposits in Moldovan
lei placed with other banks.
Interbank
loan/deposit
market
(transactions in MDL)
In 2017, like in the previous year, banks were
passive on the interbank loan/deposit market
due to excess liquidity in the banking system.
Thus, in the reporting year, transactions were
recorded only in March and April, which were
performed by a small number of participants
(3 banks) and for overnight maturity only. The
volume of transactions amounted to 60.0
million lei,
compared to 272.0 million lei
recorded in 2016.
The average annual interest rate, following
the downward trend set by the NBM monetary
policy rates, decreased by 7.29 p.p. compared
to the previous year, having recorded 9.00%
per year.
52


3.6
Forex market
Evolution of forex market in the Republic
of Moldova
In 2017, the turnover of transactions carried
out against Moldovan lei (MDL)16
on the
domestic forex market recorded increases,
both in total volume and in the volume of each
counterpart.
The total turnover of traded currencies against
Moldovan lei amounted to USD 12 007.6
million, increasing by 12.5%, compared to the
previous year (Table A.9).
Foreign currency
purchases
increased
at
a
faster
pace,
amounting to USD 6 061.7 million (+13.1%),
whereas foreign currency sales totalled USD
5 945.9 million, rising by 11.9%, compared to
2016.

domestic

market
kept
increasing,
eventually recording 31.8% (Chart 3.33).
As before, individuals preferred to apply to
foreign exchange bureaux of licensed banks
for foreign exchange services.
In 2017, the
volume of transactions carried out through
licensed banks recorded an increase of 7.1%,
accounting for a share of 64.8% in the total
turnover of the cash forex market. Similarly, a
signiﬁcant increase (35.2%) was recorded in
the volume of transactions carried out through
foreign exchange ofﬁces.
An upturn in the trade with the EU countries as
well as remittances from these countries
determined the growth of the euro share in the
total turnover of the forex market by 3.0 pp.,
to 48.2% (Chart 3.35). Thus, for the ﬁrst time,
the single European currency surpassed the
US dollar (44.6%), becoming the most traded
currency on the local market. Analysing the
cash forex market by currency structure, one
can note that the largest share was also
accounted for by the euro (Table A.10).
53


Alongside
the
increase
in
the
volume
of
transactions
carried
out
in
the
euro,
a
signiﬁcant increase in the number of these
transactions was also recorded.
Thus, the
number
of
EUR/MDL
currency
exchange
transactions carried out by legal entities rose
by about 21.0%, compared to the previous
year, their share amounting to 64.0% (Chart
3.36).

The foreign currency sales of licensed banks
recorded
similar
dynamics
to
these
of
purchases, reaching the equivalent of USD
5 321.5 million in 2017. The foreign currency
demand
came
mainly
from
legal
entities
(76.4% of total sales), the volume of sales to
corporate
clients
increasing
by
11.4%,
compared to 2016.

individuals, both in cash (including through

and
by

transfer.
Given the above, in 2017, local banks recorded
net sales of foreign currency to legal entities
in the amount of USD 1 657.5 million, which
were amply covered by net purchases of foreign
currency from individuals (including through
foreign exchange ofﬁces), totalling USD 1 887.2
million.
The foreign currency surplus, including from
other sources,
was absorbed through the
54


NBM’s purchase interventions amounting to
USD 433.8 million (Table A.11).
Evolution of foreign currency assets and
liabilities of licensed banks
During 2017, the balance of foreign currency
liabilities on the balance sheet of licensed
banks increased as a result of the rise in
placements made in personal foreign currency
accounts as well as in the volume of external
loans received by local banks. As a result, the
balance sheet assets also increased, especially
the liquid assets (Chart 3.39).

During 2017,
the licensed banks’ foreign
currency lending recorded a moderate rise.
The share of the balance of foreign currency
loans did not exceed 53.1% in foreign currency
assets, recording at the end of the reporting
year a share of 46.9%, down by 6.7 pp.,
compared to 2016.
A 5.8% growth of the balance of foreign
currency
loans
extended
by
local
banks,
amounting to USD 803.0 million, can be solely
attributed to exchange differences resulting
from the appreciation of the euro against the
US dollar on foreign markets.
By excluding
exchange differences, one can note that the
evolution of the balance of foreign currency
loans resulted in a 2.8% decline in the banks’
foreign currency lending, the balance of loans
extended in the US dollars,
in particular,
decreasing by 10.0% (Table A.12). At the same
time, the balance of loans extended in euros
slightly increased, by 0.9%. In the currency
structure of the balance of loans, a share of
slightly above two-thirds of total loans was
held by loans extended in euros, the rest of
loans being extended in the US dollars.
At the end of the reporting year, the portfolio
of currency-linked loans (USD 68.5 million)
accounted for 4.0% of total foreign currency
assets of licensed banks. The balance of these
loans
increased
by
12.9%,
compared
to
December
31,
2016,
the
increase
being
supported by the euro-linked loans.
During 2017, banks tended to accumulate
excessive foreign currency liquidity in order to
ensure that their customers beneﬁt from
smooth current settlements. At the end of the
reporting year, the available foreign currency
funds accounted for 40.7% of total foreign
currency assets, occasionally recording even
higher shares (43.3%) over the year.
As of
55


December 31, 2017, the foreign currency
balance of local banks totalled USD 697.6
million, increasing by 40.4%, year-on-year, or,
by excluding exchange differences, by 29.8%.
The balances of all types of liquidity recorded
increases, in particular, the balance of funds
held in "Nostro" accounts opened in foreign
banks (Table A.13).
At the end of 2017, the foreign currency
balance of licensed banks had the following
structure: "Nostro" accounts opened abroad -
66.5%,
foreign
currency
cash
-
17.9%,
placements abroad - 8.9%, overnight deposits -
6.5 % and foreign currency securities - 0.2%.
Regarding the currency structure of available
foreign currency funds held by local banks, as
of December 31, 2017, the largest share was
held by the euro - 66.5%, followed by the US
dollar - 30.2%, the Russian rubble - 1.6% and
other currencies - 1.7%.
Following a steady increase in the volume of
foreign currency deposits placed with the
banks,
the
balance
of
foreign
currency
required reserves, as of December 31, 2017,
totalled USD 212.1 million,
increasing by
23.5%, compared to the end of 2016, and
accounting for 12.4% of total foreign currency
assets on balance sheet.
At the end of the reference year, the balance
of foreign currency liabilities on the balance
sheet of licensed banks amounted to USD
1 699.4
million,
increasing
by
20.5%
or,
excluding exchange differences, by 10.4%,
compared to the end of 2016 (Chart 3.41).
This
increase
was
mainly
fuelled
by
placements in euros, which grew by 27.3%, of
which 15.8 pp. were accounted for by positive
exchange
differences
resulting
from
the
appreciation of the euro against the US dollar
on foreign markets. In terms of the currency
structure of foreign currency liabilities, as of 31
December 2017, the single European currency
strengthened
its
position
by
3.7
pp.,
accounting for 67.6% (Chart 3.42).
Such basic liabilities as term deposits, various
deposits and current accounts of non-bank
clients (hereinafter – foreign currency accounts
of non-bank clients) continued to hold a
signiﬁcant share of 87.9% in the portfolio of
foreign currency liabilities of licensed banks
(Table A.14). By the end of 2017, the balance
of foreign currency accounts of non-bank
clients totalled USD 1 493.4 million, increasing
by 17.7%, or, excluding exchange differences,
by 7.8%, compared to the end of 2016.

dollar-denominated accounts, the share of

decreased
almost
in

by
10.1%,
thus

The

was

for

individuals
showed
no
preference
for
a
particular
type
of

therefore

for
62.2%
of
their
total
foreign
currency
56


liabilities on the balance sheet.
At the end of the reporting year, the balance of
foreign
currency
accounts
of
non-bank
corporate clients (USD 436.3 million) recorded
a share of 25.7% in total foreign currency
liabilities on the balance sheet of licensed
banks, thus marking an increase of 10.9%, or,
by excluding exchange differences, of 2.7%,
compared to the end of 2016, due to net
inﬂows of the US dollars.
In 2017, foreign currency term deposits placed
by non-bank clients (particularly individuals)
continued to be the main source of ﬁnancing
for the banks’ foreign currency lending activity.
As of December 31, 2017, the balance of term
deposits recorded a share of 47.1% in total
foreign currency liabilities on the balance
sheet and covered almost entirely (99.6%) the
balance of foreign currency loans extended by
licensed banks.
In 2017,
raising loans by licensed banks
exceeded loan repayments and, after three
consecutive year of decline, the balance of the
loans raised increased by 26.6% (exchange
differences
excluded),
amounting
to
USD
158.6 million. However, the number of raised
foreign currency loans remains low, especially
in the case of loans raised from abroad, the
balance of which at the end of 2017 recorded
a level equal to 1/3 of the balance of loans
raised in 2016.
At the end of the reporting year, the licensed
banks’
foreign
currency
liabilities
on
the
balance
sheet
against
non-residents
accounted for 11.0%, compared to 9.6%, as of
31 December 2016.
The balance of contingent assets and liabilities
held in foreign currency by licensed banks
recorded, at the end of 2017, a decrease of
USD 55.4 million or 46.9%, compared to 31
December 2016 (Table A.16).
At the end of
2017,
contingent
assets
and
liabilities
in
foreign currency recorded roughly the same
values, reﬂecting the local banks’ commitment
to
carry
out
mainly
currency-to-currency
conversion operations.
The value of the total open (long / short)
foreign exchange position recorded by licensed
banks at the end of 2017 increased by USD 8.3
million, reaching a level of 2.21% in the total
regulatory
capital
TRC.
Compared
to
December 31, 2016, foreign currency positions
increased in all currencies, yet mostly in the
US dollar and the euro.

On average, the Moldovan leu appreciated
against the US dollar and the single European
currency by 7.2% and 5.6%,
respectively,
compared to 2016.
In 2017, the currencies of most major trading
partner countries, accounting for signiﬁcant
shares
in
the
Moldovan
foreign
trade,
strengthened their positions against the US
dollar. According to the quotations at the end
of the year, the Polish zloty and the Czech
crown appreciated more strongly against the
US dollar than the Moldovan leu, their price
rising by 17.0% and 16.9%, respectively (Chart
3.44).
57


At the same time, the real effective exchange
rate19
(REER)
of
the
domestic
currency
appreciated by 13.4%. The following countries
particularly contributed to the strengthening,
in real terms, of the Moldovan leu: Romania -
by 3.0 pp., Russia - by 2.3 pp. and Ukraine - by
1.5 pp. (Charts 3.45 and 3.46).
In 2017, the exchange rate assumed a steady
appreciation trend, as the situation in the forex
market was similar to that of 2016, when
economy recorded a signiﬁcant surplus of
foreign currency.

Economic growth in the host countries of
Moldovan
labour
migrants
had
positive
implications for foreign currency remittances
from abroad, which, after two years of decline,
returned to positive values,
increasing by
11.2%. As a result, the net foreign currency
supply from individuals, mainly of euros, grew
by 4.8%, compared to 2016, amounting to
USD 1 982.6 million (Chart 3.47).
In 2017, foreign trade operations also recorded
an upturn, the growth rate of imports of goods
(20.2%20) exceeding that of exports of goods
(18.6%20). Given that the trade balance deﬁcit
is not covered only through sources available
in
the
market
(such
as
foreign
currency
purchases), but also through commercial loans,
foreign loans, etc., its deterioration by 21.8%
did not put pressures on the local forex market.
In this context,
the net foreign currency
demand from economic agents increased by as
little as 5.2%, totalling USD 1 657.5 million. By
disaggregating this indicator, it was observed
that economic agents that were primarily
involved in exports sold 16.1% more currency
to banks, the supply of foreign currency mainly
19Calculated based on the share of main trading partners
and the average exchange rates, December 2000=100.
20According to data published by the National Bureau of
Statistics.
coming from producers of cereal and oil
products, nuts and dried fruit, sugar, etc. At
the same time, economic agents that were
primarily involved in imports purchased 11.4%
more currency than in 2016. Foreign currency
demand from energy importers increased by
8.2%, only due to an increased demand from
importers of oil / fuel products.

Under these circumstances, the net foreign
currency supply from individuals exceeded
(119.6%), for the second consecutive year and
in similar proportions, the net foreign currency
demand from economic agents.
Unlike in 2016, in 2017, the condition of
foreign
currency
surplus
was
recorded
monthly, except for February (Chart 3.48). In
order to restore the market’s equilibrium and
to avoid unsustainable appreciation of the
Moldovan leu, the NBM had to intervene on the
market, roughly monthly, adjusting the volume
of
its
foreign
currency
purchases
to
the
amount of surplus currency available on the
market.
In 2017, the NBM purchased USD
58


433.8 million, thus improving the resilience of
the foreign exchange reserves of the state.
Speaking of seasonal trends, the net foreign
exchange supply from individuals retained its
previous
seasonal
pattern,
gradually
increasing up to the third quarter when it
reached its peak level ever since 2014 of USD
660.1 million. Atypical developments in supply
were recorded in the fourth quarter of the year,
when foreign currency surplus kept persisting
on
the
market.
This
development
was
determined, on the one hand, by stronger
purchases of foreign currency by banks from
agricultural exporters and, on the other hand,
by a lower demand from the energy importers,
taking into account that some of them have
accumulated the required amount of foreign
currency in previous quarters.

At the end of 2017, ofﬁcial reserve assets

goods and services (around 5.5 months of
imports22).
The management of foreign exchange
reserves
In accordance with art. 5, 16 and 53 of the Law
on the National Bank of Moldova, the NBM
keeps and manages foreign exchange reserves
of the state, carries out foreign exchange
transactions
by
using
foreign
exchange
reserves, and maintains them at a level which
is appropriate for the implementation of the
monetary and foreign exchange policy of the
state. The role of foreign exchange reserves is
to ensure the credibility and ﬁnancial stability
of the country, to honour payments related to
the external liabilities of the state, and to cope
with unpredictable exogenous shocks.
When managing foreign exchange reserves,
the NBM ensures the high level of security and
the required level of liquidity of investments.
The NBM’s investment policy is characterised
by prudence and aims to optimise proﬁtability,
provided that the investments’ liquidity and
security requirements are met. An essential
element of the foreign reserves’ management
is the investment risk’s management, which
is carried out through constraints and limits
imposed on investments.
Table A.17 lists the main risks associated with
the management of foreign exchange reserves
and the methods used to mitigate them.
Starting with July 2013, the NBM, under the
cooperation
with
the
World
Bank,
began
applying
the
strategic
asset
allocation
approach (SAA). The strategic asset allocation
involves a long-term plan of action for asset
management
aimed
at
achieving
optimal
levels of proﬁtability and risk.
In the SAA
context, international reserves are divided into
three classes (current, liquidity and investment
22Calculated based on the latest forecasts available at
the time of writing this report on the import of goods and
services in 2018.
reserves), according to their speciﬁc objectives
and regulations, making this approach more
effective in achieving the established goal.
The
foreign
reserves’
management
is
conditioned
by
the
evolution
of
global
economy, the decisions of the major central
banks of the world, especially of the monetary
authorities of the US, the Eurozone and the UK,
as well as by the shares held by the currencies
of these countries in the structure of ofﬁcial
reserve assets.
For
the
foreign
exchange
reserves’
management
the
NBM
chooses
safe
instruments, employed by other central banks,
such
as:
placements
on
correspondent
accounts (usually with other central banks),
foreign
currency
term
deposits
and
placements in three classes of securities:
supranational
securities
(issued
by
supranational
institutions),
government
securities (issued by the US government,
governments of the EU Member States, other
high-rated
state-guaranteed
government
issuers)
and
non-governmental
securities
(issued by high-rated agencies).

and investment management services, signed
between

for

and

In 2017, the portfolio of securities held to
maturity remained unchanged.
As of 31

amounted to USD 411.53 million, accounting
for 14.70% of total foreign exchange reserves.
According to the decision no. 286 of December
23, 2014 of the Council of Administration of
the NBM, with effect from January 1, 2015, the
share of the held-to-maturity securities shall
not exceed 20% of total foreign exchange
reserves.

According to the data collected at the end of
the reporting year, 47.9% of the reserves were
invested in securities, 39.8% represent term
deposits, and 12.3% are kept as cash and funds
in corresponding accounts (Chart 3.50 ).
Return on investment depends on market
conditions, the exchange rate ﬂuctuations and
the prices of investment instruments.
The
evolution of the global economic environment
and the prospects for the development of
ﬁnancial markets inﬂuenced the investment
decisions and the currency composition of the
reserves. In this context, the largest share in
the foreign currency reserves structure was
allocated to the US dollar - 73.6% (Chart 3.51).
At the same time, the currency composition of
the ofﬁcial reserves falls within the established
deviation allowance of +/- 10% from the
regulatory currency composition (Chart 3.52).
In spite of the negative investment return
rates
on
the
short-
and
medium-term
euro-denominated
investment
instruments,
the foreign exchange management decisions
taken by the NBM led, in 2017, to a proﬁt of
USD 28.11 million, an average proﬁt rate
recording a level of 1.14%.
61


Chapter 4
Banking supervision
4.1
Evolution
of
the
banking sector
Banking sector, including core indicators
As of 31 December 2017, 11 banks licensed by
the National Bank of Moldova were operating
in
the
Republic
of
Moldova,
including
4
subsidiaries of
foreign banks
and foreign
ﬁnancial groups.
The total number of subdivisions of licensed
banks in the Republic of Moldova by the
31.12.2017 constituted 794, of which 294
branches and 500 agencies.
The number of workers employed in the
banking sector, as of 31 December 2017,
represented 7 873 individuals.
On average,
assets under management per each employee
in the banking sector represented an amount
of MDL 10.1 million, recording an increase of
MDL 0.8 million (9.1%), compared to the end
of 2016.
The ﬁnancial situation of the banking sector
by the 31.12.2017 was satisfactory. The banks
are sufﬁciently capitalized, capital constituting
MDL 10 106.7 million, and the risk-weighted
capital adequacy indicator reached a high level
of 31.0% and is maintained by all banks. Both
indicators have been on the rise compared to
the end of 201623. Dynamics of main indicators
is shown in the chart 4.1.
The proﬁt for the ﬁnancial year ending on
31.12.2017 for the banking sector constituted
MDL 1 480 million, recording an increase of
MDL 116.7 million (8.6%), compared to the
previous year. For the year 2017, the return on
assets24 and the return on capital25, of the
licensed banks amounted to 1.8% and 11.1%,
23The data by 31.12.2016 throughout the text are
adjusted according to the results of the external audit.
24Return on assets = annualized net income / average
assets
25Return on capital = annualized net income / average
share capital
respectively, being at the same level as at the
end of the previous year.
At the same time, the loan portfolio decreased
by MDL 1 288.0 million (3.7%) during the year,
amounting to MDL 33 473.3 million.
The
reduction of the portfolio was inﬂuenced by
the
current
challenging
economic
developments that led to lower demand for
credit
as
well
as
to
a
more
risk-averse
approach by banks to potential borrowers.
We note that, following the creation of the
Credit Risk Register, the NBM daily monitors
the quality of the loan portfolio, including
applications for loans etc., the banks being
motivated to be more cautious in accepting
transactions and adequately assessing the
risks.

risks related to assets in banks’ balance sheets.
Banks continued to comply with prudential
liquidity requirements imposed by the NBM,
which
reveal
the
existence
of
adequate
sources of funding for covering potential needs
in both the short and the long term.
The balance of deposits grew by MDL 5 058.3
62


million (9.2%) during the year 2017 up to MDL
59 896.9 million.

National Bank of Moldova.

of

have

risk-weighted capital adequacy ratio (the limit

medium banks and of small banks decreased
accordingly by 1.4 p.p. and 0.2 p.p.

To evaluate the banking sector concentration
in
the
Republic
of
Moldova,
the
Herﬁndahl-Hirschmann index was calculated26.
The HH index is used as a possible indicator of
market power or of competition between
companies (the value of which does not
exceed 10 000), based on the bank’s share of
assets in total assets in the banking sector.
This indicator is calculated as the sum of
squares of market quotas of all banks in the
sector. In international practice, the following
division is used:
• HHI under 100 indicates a market with
perfect competition;
• HHI between 100 and 1 500 indicates an
unconcentrated market;
• HHI between 1 500 and 2 500 indicates a
moderate concentration;
• HHI
over
2
500
indicates
a
high
concentration.
Thus, based on calculations performed and
obtaining HHI = 1 723.7, we conclude that
the banking sector of the Republic of Moldova
represents a moderate concentration.
26Market concentration index, i.e. the extent to which a
small number of enterprises represent a large part of the
market.

• available for sale ﬁnancial assets - by MDL

• tax receivables - by MDL 15.9 million

6.8 million (4.0%) to MDL 165.0 million.
The total amount of deposits in proportion to
GDP
decreased
insigniﬁcantly
by
0.3
p.p.
compared to the previous year, accounting for
40.3% at the reporting date.
68


As of 31.12.2017, deposits in foreign currency
as a percentage of total deposits represented
42.8% (the equivalent of MDL 25 632.3 million),
their absolute value increased by MDL 148.5
million (0.6%), compared to the end of the
previous year. The deposits increased due to
an increase in attracted funds, by MDL 1 813.4
million, and decreased due to the revaluation
as a result of exchange rate ﬂuctuations, by
MDL 1 683.8 million (the calculation of the
revaluation of deposits was made based on
EUR, USD, Russian rubble, Romanian leu and
Ukrainian hryvnia, the share of other currencies
being insigniﬁcant). Deposits in Moldovan lei
amounted to 57.2% of total deposits, or MDL
34 264.6 million, and represented an increase
of MDL 4 909.9 million (16.7%), as compared
to 31.12.2016.

Sensitivity to market risk
The proportion of the foreign currency assets
aggregated with the assets attached to foreign
currency exchange rate in total assets at
31.12.2017 constituted 36.8%. The proportion
of the foreign currency balance sheet liabilities
and of bonds attached to the foreign currency
exchange rate in total assets on the same date
amounted
to
36.5%.
The
insigniﬁcant
difference of these weights indicates that the
direct currency risk is insigniﬁcant.
Placements abroad by banks of the Republic of
Moldova as of 31.12.2017, according to data
presented by licensed banks, constituted MDL
9 782.8 million, recording an increase of MDL
1 984.9 million (25.5%), compared to the end
of the previous year, and representing 12.3%
of total bank assets and 96.8% of Tier I capital.
Details
are
shown
in
Table
A.20.
Thus,
placements in Germany registered the largest
increase of MDL 2 380,5 million (259.0%).
According to the situation on 31.12.2017, the
largest share of total placements in the sector
was in Germany - 33.7%, USA - 21.7%, Belgium
- 16.6% and France - 12.3%, the details are
presented in the chart 4.16.
According to the Regulation on classiﬁcation of
contingent assets and liabilities (MO of RM No
216-221 of 09.12.2011), banks reduce their
capital by the amount of potential losses,
taking into account the rating assigned by
international agencies by countries and by
banks.

Shareholding modiﬁcation
During the year 2017, the National Bank of
Moldova authorized access to the banking
market of the Republic of Moldova to two
signiﬁcant banks of the European Union:
(i) Intesa Sanpaolo S.p.A. (Italy) - a banking
institution of systemic importance for the
European Union supervised by the Central
Bank of Europe, which is the parent bank of
the Intesa Sanpaolo Group, with a signiﬁcant
presence in Central and Eastern Europe, the
Middle East and North Africa, which acquired
the integral share package issued by BC
“EXIMBANK - Gruppo Veneto Banca” SA;
(ii) Banca Transilvania S.A. (Romania) - the
second largest bank of the banking sector in
Romania
and
the
parent
bank
of
Banca
Transilvania Financial Group,
which has a
signiﬁcant presence on the ﬁnancial services
market in Romania, as well as in the Republic
of Moldova in the ﬁeld of ﬁnancial leasing,
which acquired the control package in the
share capital of BC “VICTORIABANK” S.A.
4.2
The
NBM
supervisory
activity
Throughout
2017,
the
National
Bank
of
Moldova continued to promote reforms in the
banking
sector,
focusing
in
particular
on
establishing
a
transparent
shareholder
structure
in
order
to
attract
new
and
appropriate investors, on proper assessment
of the bank’s management, on identiﬁcation of
transactions concluded with afﬁliated banks
and
on
the
timely
recognition
of
non-performing loans.
These functions are
being carried out through both on-site and ex
ofﬁcio controls, using the supervisory tools
available at the National Bank.
On-site controls
In 2017, there were performed 8 complex
inspections at licensed banks.
As a result of inspections, sanctions were
imposed on 4 banks: sanctioned with warnings
- 1 bank;
10 members of the Board,
14
70


members of the Committee; sanctioned with
ﬁnes -15 members of the Committee;
33
prescriptions.
Decisions with application of sanctions and
prescriptions have been adopted as a result of
the ﬁnding of violations and deﬁciencies,
mainly related to the following:
• the administration of the bank was not
performed in strict compliance with the
requirements of the law and regulatory
acts issued by the NBM;
• the
positions’
requirements
for
risk-weighted assets were not met;
• the
limits
and
requirements
for
risk
concentration (exposures) have not been
met);
• engaging the bank in risky and doubtful
operations.
At the same time, it is important to mention
that in the reporting year, 293 inspections
were performed, including 260 planned and 33
unannounced inspections. As a result of these
inspections, 64 currency exchange entities
were warned,
the activity of 20 currency
exchange units was suspended, ﬁnes were
applied to 18 currency exchange units and
license was withdrawn from one currency
exchange unit.
Ex-ofﬁcio controls
Following the implementation of the Credit
Risk Register, the National Bank is receiving
detailed,
reliable
and
prompt
information
related to credit risk to which licensed banks
are exposed.
Availability of complex operative data allows
the central bank to determine the respective
risk not only by sector but also by groups of
banks or by individual bank.
It contributes
to the early identiﬁcation of risks associated
with credit portfolios, ensuring a process of
analytical and interpretative investigation of
the corrective actions taken by the National
Bank. Accordingly, the NBM monitors on a daily
basis the quality of the loan portfolios, including
requests for granting, classiﬁcation of credits
granted to regulated requirements, etc.
In
case of classiﬁcation of credits contrary to the
regulations in force, the NBM requests through
letters to remove the detected violations. Thus,
banks are motivated to become more cautious
in accepting transactions and to assess risks
adequately.
During 2017, as a result of monitoring of the
loan portfolios, the process of increasing the
share of non-performing loans was ascertained.
In this context, in the ﬁrst quarter of 2017, the
National Bank repeatedly recommended banks
to apply appropriate governance measures, to
have
effective
processes
for
identifying,
monitoring, managing and reporting the risks
to
which
they
may
be
exposed,
and
to
strengthen internal control procedures,
in
particular those related to monitoring and
management of non-performing loans. Thus,
banks have developed strategies or plans to
mitigate
non-performing
loans
and
are
constantly undertaking concrete measures to
improve the quality of loan portfolios.
In order to ensure transparency of the banking
sector in the Republic of Moldova, the National
Bank,
as
a
regulatory
and
supervisory
authority, monitors the ownership structure of
banks, veriﬁes their shareholders to determine
suitability of shareholders to the requirements
of the Law on banking activity and of the
Regulation on holding participation quotas in
the bank’s share capital, as well as in order to
ensure
certainty
in
identifying
effective
beneﬁciaries by implementing the following:
• permanent tracking of transactions with
banks’ shares;
• requesting presentation of information
related to the identity and activity of
potential purchasers, including direct and
indirect
quota
holders,
to
actual
beneﬁciaries,
and
submission
of
questionnaires, additional documents /
information necessary to assess their
quality,
including
presentation
of
information in the event of modiﬁcations
on previously presented data to the NBM;
• conducting inquiries on how to acquire
bank’s shares,
as well as requesting
support of competent authorities;
• analysis
of
information
/
documents,
available
media
sources
containing
information about banks’ shareholders
During
2017,
remedial
measures
and
sanctions were applied to shareholders of two
banks. The Executive Board of the National
Bank on the 14 of March 2017 decided to
impose a ﬁne to a direct and indirect holder of
the substantial quota in the capital of BC
“VICTORIABANK” S.A. for non-observance of
requirements
of
the
Law
on
ﬁnancial
institutions
regarding
presentation
of
71


information and documents requested by the
National Bank for the purposes of supervision
and evaluation of the quality of the bank’s
shareholders.
The ﬁne was applied in the
amount of about 1% of the share held in the
share capital of BC “VICTORIABANK” S.A. by
the direct and indirect holder.
Also, the Executive Board of the National Bank,
on 21.09.2017, decided to sanction by ﬁne a
direct holder of substantial quota in the capital
of the “Bank of Finance and Commerce” S.A.
for non-observance of requirements of the Law
on ﬁnancial institutions for presentation of
information and documents requested by the
National Bank for the purposes of supervision
and evaluation of the quality of the bank’s
shareholders.
The ﬁne was applied in the
amount of 1% of the participation quota held
by the shareholder in the bank’s share capital
and was paid into the state budget.
Banking
supervision
measures
meant
to
increase the ﬁnancial sector’s resilience have
intensiﬁed. Out of 11 banks, 7 were subject to
complex on-site inspections.
Two systemic
banks were under intensive supervision and
one bank was in early intervention regime.
The
National
Bank
conducted
thematic
inspections on shareholders’ transparency and
quality, on veriﬁcation of transactions with
related parties, on assets’ quality assessments,
as well as other thematic controls.
As a result of adoption on October 4, 2016 of
the Law on recovery and resolution of banks
no. 232 of 03.10.2016, published in the Ofﬁcial
Monitor
of
the
Republic
of
Moldova
no.
343-346 art. 707 of 04.10.2016, according to
art.
9 of the speciﬁed Law, the NBM has
requested banks to develop and maintain a
recovery plan that sets out the measures to be
taken to restore their ﬁnancial position in case
of signiﬁcant deterioration. At the same time,
in order to facilitate the implementation of the
recovery plan, the NBM has developed and
submitted to banks a Guide on development of
a
recovery
plan
containing
detailed
information on all aspects that need to be
included in the plan.
In this context, in the
third
quarter
of
2017,
as
a
result
of
examination
of
completeness
of
recovery
plans, letters were sent to banks indicating
elimination of drawbacks and submission to
NBM of updated recovery plans. In the fourth
quarter of 2017, the banks submitted to the
NBM
revised
recovery
plans,
following
comments
previously
received
from
the
National Bank.
In
the
context
of
transition
to
BASEL
III
standards, the NBM has solicited the licensed
banks to develop the implementation strategy
for
the
Basel
III
Agreement,
including
alignment to the provisions of the Law on
banking activity and the Regulation on the
bank’s activity management framework. The
banks report regularly, every 2 months, on the
progress made, the results achieved and the
actions taken.
Thus, banks have set up
working groups on implementation of Basel III
framework and have concluded contracts with
audit ﬁrms to provide specialized assistance.
At the same time, in order to assess the
adequacy of capital and the ability of banks to
report
under
the
new
framework,
NBM
resumed the Quantitative Impact Study (QIS),
which stated that all banks in the Republic of
Moldova have sufﬁcient capital to meet the
new prudential requirements.
At the same
time, in 2017, the National Bank of Moldova
initiated the identiﬁcation, with the assistance
of a foreign audit company, of transactions
concluded with related parties of the B.C.
“MOLDOVA-AGROINDBANK”
S.A.,
B.C.
“VICTORIABANK”
S.A.
and
BC
“Moldindconbank” S.A. These banks are in
breach of prudential limits and will have up to
2 years to comply.
Intensive
supervision
and
early
intervention
In
2017,
two
banks
(BC
“MOLDOVA-AGROINDBANK”
S.A.
and
BC
"VICTORIABANK" S.A.) continued to be under
the intensive supervision regime and one bank
(BC "Moldindconbank" S.A.) under the early
intervention regime.
The mandate of the
temporary
administrators
of
BC
"Moldindconbank" S.A. have been prolonged
several times during 2017.
In order to avoid excessive risk, the National
Bank monitors daily the activity of banks
under intensive supervision and under the
early intervention regime. In such a way, the
ﬁnancial
situation
of
these
banks,
the
transactions, the agenda of the meetings of
the governing bodies, etc. are examined.
The speciﬁed banks are well capitalized and
operate in normal regime (they provide all
services, including those related to deposits,
credits and settlement).
In 2017, the NBM carried out a range of
complex
inspections,
including
at
the
aforementioned banks. Following the results of
inspections performed, the Executive Board of
the NBM approved decisions of imposing
72


sanctions on the management of banks. At the
same
time,
banks
have
been
prescribed
remedial measures to optimize their lending
and customer due diligence procedures, to
improve regulatory standards in the ﬁeld of
credit and internal control, to improve the
security management system, the internal
control system and the payment system, etc.
The banks have developed remedial action
plans for liquidation of infringements and
drawbacks found during inspections and will
report to the National Bank during the 2018 on
their implementation.
The ﬁndings made by the National Bank of
Moldova regarding two groups of shareholders
of
the
BC
"MOLDOVA-AGROINDBANK"
S.A.
which
acted
in
concert
and
acquired
a
qualifying holding in the bank’s share capital in
the
size
of
43.1%,
without
prior
written
permission of the National Bank,these groups
intended to sell the acquired shares within 3
months. Because the mentioned shares were
not alienated within the set deadlines, the
shares were cancelled and new ones were
issued.
Thus, on June 1, 2017, two unique
packages of shares were put on sale at the
Moldova
Stock
Exchange.
The
National
Commission
for
Financial
Markets
has
repeatedly extended the term of sale of new
shares issued by the bank.
It is worth
mentioning
that
as
of
29.12.2017
the
amendments to the Law on capital market no.
171
of
July
11,
2012,
by
which
it
was
established the procedure for cancellation,
issuance and sale of participation quotas held
in breach of the requirements on the quality of
shareholders’ equity in the capital of banks,
the period of exposure for sale having been
extended.
With reference to BC "Moldindconbank" S.A.,
according to provisions of the Law on ﬁnancial
institutions, the persons identiﬁed by the NBM
as acting in concert without prior written
permission of the NBM were supposed to
alienate voluntarily the shares within 3 months.
After ﬁnalization of legal actions related to
safeguarding
measures
of
shares,
as
of
October
2017,
the
aforementioned
shareholders had the possibility to alienate the
shares held.
Banks in liquidation
As of 31.12.2017, 8 banks were placed in
forced liquidation process. During 2017, the
National
Bank
of
Moldova
approved
the
Reports
on
completion
of
the
liquidation
process of BC “Basarabia” and BC “Bancosind”
S.A.
Regarding the liquidation process of Banca de
Economii S.A., BC “UNIBANK” S.A. and BC
“BANCA SOCIAL˘A” SA, initiated in 2015, we
mention that, with the purpose of recovering
the funds of these banks, in the year 2017 the
liquidators
cooperated
with
the
law
enforcement bodies, undertook organizational
measures regarding sale of assets owned and
in possession of banks and continued work
with debtors of banks. As a result, at the end
of 2017, 179 actions were in progress in courts,
in order to recover funds previously disbursed
by banks, in total value of MDL 7,440.0 million.
At the same time, the investigation bodies
were
managing
44
criminal
cases
ﬁled
according to liquidators’ notiﬁcations.
As a result of the actions undertaken by
liquidators, during the liquidation process, up
to date funds in a total amount of MDL 1 002.6
million were recovered, including in 2017 –
MDL 306.6 million (Banca de Economii SA –
MDL 159.4 million, BC “BANCA SOCIAL˘A” SA –
MDL 134.4 million and BC “UNIBANK” SA – MDL
12.8 million).
The ﬁnancial resources recovered during the
process of liquidation of banks were used for
the payment of debts related to emergency
loans granted to Banca de Economii S.A., BC
“BANCA SOCIAL˘A” S.A. and B.C. “UNIBANK”
S.A., until the appointment of liquidators of the
respective banks. Thus, until the 31.12.2017,
funds amounting to MDL 1 071.2 million were
repaid, including in 2017 – MDL 209.9 million.
Another aspect of actions related to the
recovery of the resources of the Banca de
Economii S.A., BC "BANCA SOCIAL˘A" S.A. and
B.C. "UNIBANK" S.A. represents the survey
conducted by the consortium Kroll and Steptoe
& Johnson.
On 21 December 2017,
the
National Bank of Moldova published a detailed
summary of the banking fraud investigation,
which it received together with the second
report from the consortium Kroll and Steptoe &
Johnson. The synthesis reveals the way the
money
was
embezzled,
as
well
as
the
countries were they circulated or arrived. The
document has eight chapters that reﬂect the
international investigative methodology, the
analysis of fraudulent banks’ exposures in
2012-2014, Shor’s group link to fraudulent
banks’
ownership,
money
laundering
mechanisms, and the destination of fraudulent
funds.
The National Bank of Moldova analysed the
draft report and requested a few clariﬁcations,
73


provided
comments
and
suggestions
to
representatives of Kroll and Steptoe & Johnson.
Consequently, on March 6, 2018, the National
Bank of Moldova received the ﬁnal report on
the banking fraud investigation from Kroll and
Steptoe & Johnson.
The document contains
information
about
the
fraud
that
led
to
insolvency of “Banca de Economii” S.A., BC
"BANCA SOCIAL˘A" S.A. and B.C. "UNIBANK"
S.A.
The ﬁnal report will be used by the National
Bank of Moldova for the purpose of identifying
shortcomings in the system and implementing
the appropriate measures. At the same time,
following the visit of Kroll representatives to
the Republic of Moldova, the NBM ofﬁcially
received all the investigation papers from the
international consortium Kroll and Steptoe &
Johnson, which were forwarded to the Anti-
Corruption Prosecutor’s Ofﬁce.
Representatives of Kroll and Steptoe & Johnson
have conﬁrmed their willingness to work with
investigation bodies to support efforts to
recover internationally fraudulent funds and to
strengthen
the
evidence
base
within
the
procedures at national level.
4.3
Banking
sector
risks’
assessment
In order to monitor risks in the banking sector,
the National Bank of Moldova performs stress
tests, which are supervisory tools used to
assess the strengths of each bank, as well as
the overall resistance to shocks of the banking
sector. The stress tests used by the National
Bank focus on credit risk, liquidity risk, interest
rate risk and exchange rate risk as well as on
combinations of these.
All stress tests are
based on the information reported by licensed
banks and are applied across the banking
sector, by groups of banks and of individual
banks.
As a result of the stress tests performed at the
level of the banking sector, it was found that
banks
in
the
Republic
of
Moldova
are
sufﬁciently capitalized and capable to cope
with most of simulated shocks. Exceptions are
scenarios
where
the
likelihood
of
severe
shocks or so-called historical peaks affect
credit risk. Under these circumstances, it is
possible that the solvency ratios of individual
banks fall below the limits regulated by the
National Bank.
However, the probability of
such scenarios materializing is very low. At the
same time, banks take the necessary steps to
improve the quality of credit portfolios, which
will allow them to reduce losses in case of
excessive stress scenario.
With regard to liquidity risk, stress test results
conﬁrm the high level of liquidity in the
banking sector, the banks being able to cope
with
deposits’
withdrawals
at
historical
maximum rates, and liquidity deﬁcits for stress
test scenarios applied were not recorded.
Also, in order to assess the potential risks that
could affect the stability of the sector and each
bank in the capital categories, asset quality,
income, liquidity and market risk sensitivity,
the National Bank conducts the study on
identifying
vulnerability
of
the
banking
sector.The study’s results indicate a low level
of vulnerability of the banking sector by the
end
of
2017
situation,
with
some
vulnerabilities recorded in the categories of
market risk sensitivity, assets’ quality and
proﬁtability.
In
order
to
capture
the
opinion
of
risk
managers of the licensed banks on major risks
to which the ﬁnancial system is exposed, the
National Bank of Moldova conducts a systemic
risk survey every six months.
Respondents
believe that key risks persist in the ﬁnancial
system, on the background of a reduction of
probability of appearing of an event with a
high impact on the system.
In order to determine the ﬁnancing conditions
and the risk proﬁle of the sectors ﬁnanced
by Moldovan banks, the NBM is conducting
quarterly a bank credit survey.
Survey results indicate a slight relaxation
trend
in
credit
standards
and
credit
agreements over the year 2017.
This trend
was largely driven by a reduction in ﬁnancing
costs and competitive pressure. At the same
time, factors such as risk perception and
tolerance have moderated the relaxation of
credit standards and contract terms, especially
for businesses. During the period considered,
the
demand
for
loans
from
businesses
remained broadly stable, while demand for
loans from the population followed an upward
trend. In the short term, banks anticipate an
increase
in
demand
for
loans
from
both
categories of borrowers.
At the same time,
respondents anticipate a slight tightening of
standards for business loans and a relaxation
of standards applied to individual loans.
74


The risk of contagion
At the end of 2017, the domestic banking
sector registered a low level of contagion risk
in terms of interbank placements both in
domestic banks and in foreign banks.
Chart 4.17 The net interbank placements of the licensed
banks in the Republic of Moldova in the domestic banking
sector
Source: NBM
Chart 4.18 The volume of net placements of licensed banks
in foreign banks
Source: NBM
The net interbank placements of the licensed
banks in the Republic of Moldova in the
domestic banking sector, by the 31.12.2017,
amounted to MDL 38.9 million, decreasing by
MDL 8.8 million compared to the same period
of the last year (Chart 4.17).
The volume of net placements of licensed
banks in foreign banks constituted, by the
31.12.2017, MDL 9 686.5 million, recording an
increase of MDL 2 036.4 million as compared
to the end of the previous year (Chart 4.18).
Out of these, 90.2% were placed in banks of
Germany, USA, Belgium, France and Austria.
4.4
Fighting
money
laundering
and
terrorist ﬁnancing
During the year 2017, the NBM assessed the
policies for identifying the risks of money
laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing in on-site
inspections
of
licensed
banks,
foreign
exchange units and non-bank payment service
providers.
At the same time, the proper
implementation
of
the
provisions
of
the
normative acts, namely the procedures for
knowing the customer, of requirements for
increased
precautions,
monitoring
of
transactions, retention of data, reporting of
transactions in accordance with the Law on
prevention and ﬁghting money laundering and
terrorist ﬁnancing no.190-XVI from 26.07.2007,
as well as the development of an efﬁcient
internal control system in this domain.
During
veriﬁcations,
the
NBM
found
shortcomings and violations of normative acts
applicable in the ﬁeld of prevention and
combating money laundering and terrorist
ﬁnancing and as a result has applied sanctions
to
supervised
institutions
and
to
their
administrators, in accordance with the Law on
ﬁnancial institutions no.550-XIII of 21.07.1995.
NBM representatives, also, contributed to the
national
process
of
identiﬁcation
and
assessment of the risks of money laundering
and terrorism ﬁnancing implemented with the
World Bank’s methodological support, process
which was completed with the publication of
the National Risk Assessment Report SB / FT
and the approval of the Action Plan on the
reduction
of
risks
in
the
ﬁeld
of
money
laundering
and
ﬁnancing
terrorism
for
2017-2019 (GD no.791 of 11.10.2017).
At
the
same
time,
the
NBM
performed
activities for implementing of an IT solution in
order to streamline the process of monitoring
shareholders’
transparency
and
remote
analysis in the ﬁeld of money laundering and
terrorism
ﬁnancing.
Thus,
there
were
developed tender speciﬁcations and other
tender documents for procurement of goods
and services. The implementation of this IT
solution will continue and in the next period by
utilizing the procurement procedure through
competitive dialogue. At the same time, the
NBM continued its actions aimed at enhancing
cooperation
and
information
exchange
75


activities
with
the
Anti-Money
Laundering
Service
and
other
national
and
overseas
institutions and authorities.
NBM endorsed the draft of a new law on
prevention and combating money laundering
and terrorism ﬁnancing, taking into account
international standards and the Directive no.
2015/849 of the European Parliament and of
the
Council
from
the
20
May
2015
on
prevention of the use of the ﬁnancial system
for the purposes of money laundering or
terrorist ﬁnancing.
76


Chapter 5
Payment System
5.1
Payment
systems
regulation
and
supervision
In accordance with the Law on the National
Bank of Moldova no. 548-XIII of 21 July 1995,
regulation and supervision of ﬁnancial market
infrastructures in the Republic of Moldova is
one of the core tasks of the National Bank.
This attribution is implemented in accordance
with the best international practices, with the
recommendations, principles and standards in
this
domain
issued
by
the
Bank
for
International
Settlements,
the
European
Central Bank, the International Monetary Fund
and the World Bank.
During 2017, modiﬁcations and completions to
the
Regulation
on
automated
interbank
payment system, approved by the Decision of
the Administrative Council of the National Bank
of Moldova no. 53, dated 02.03.2006, which
resulted from the need to adjust the legal
framework in the context of implementation of
transfer
requirement
from
the
Electronic
Governance Centre (MPay) to the Ministry of
Finance of the responsibility for performing
direct debit operations, in accordance with the
amendments
made
to
the
Government
Decision no.
280 of 24.04.2013 on certain
actions of implementation of the Government
Electronic Payments Service (MPay), as well as
of the need to adjust the respective Regulation
to the provisions of the Law on settlement
ﬁnality in payment and ﬁnancial instruments
settlement systems no. 183 of 22.07.2016.
According to the Payment System Supervision
Policy of the Republic of Moldova (approved by
the Executive Board Decision No.
299 of
October 27, 2016), the core objective of the
NBM supervision is promotion of efﬁciency and
stability of the country’s payment system. In
the
context
of
the
basic
objective,
the
elements
of
the
payment
system
of
the
Republic of Moldova are subjected to National
Bank supervision, safe and efﬁcient operation
of which is essential for ﬁnancial stability,
monetary policy implementation and public
conﬁdence in cashless payments.
In the domain of payments’ and settlement
systems’
supervision,
the
following
are
subjected to supervision:
1. Payment and settlement systems (high
value payment systems and high volume
and low value payment systems);
2. Clearing
and
settlement
systems
of
securities;
3. Money remittances;
4. Payment tools.
In exercising its oversight function, the NBM
pays special attention to systems that process,
compensate and settle high value payments
and pose a substantial risk in the event of an
operational or settlement failure. At the same
time, the NBM ensures an appropriate level of
attention to all components of the payment
system subjected to supervision.
5.2
Licensing,
regulation
and
supervision
of
payment
services
providers
and
of
electronic
money
issuers
According to the Law on payment services and
electronic money no. 114 of 18.05.2012, the
National Bank of Moldova (NBM) licenses,
regulates
and
supervises
the
activity
of
payment services providers and of electronic
money issuers.
In accordance with the nominated Law, no
license was issued/withdrawn during the year
2017 for providing payment services/electronic
money issuance activity.
As of 31.12.2017, seven non-bank payment
services providers were active in the Republic
77


of Moldova, including two payment companies,
one
postal
services
provider
and
four
companies’ issuers of electronic money.
During the year 2017, 4 on-site inspections
were performed, of which three complex and
one thematic, within which licensed non-bank
payment
services
providers
underwent
veriﬁcation.
As a result of these controls, infringements and
deﬁciencies were found related to:
• registration at the NBM of payment agents
in the Register of electronic money issuing
companies;
• identiﬁcation
of
customers
while
performing electronic transactions;
• internal procedures for prevention and
combating money laundering, which are
incomplete
and
do
not
contain
all
mandatory requirements according to
legislation;
• notiﬁcation
of
the
NBM
regarding
modiﬁcation of data in the documents
attached to the license issuing statement;
• making non-cash payments within foreign
exchange operations of individuals in the
absence of identity documents or without
indicating the purpose of the transfer etc.
Proposals
and
recommendations
were
forwarded regarding necessary actions to be
taken in order to eliminate the infringements
and drawbacks found and, where appropriate,
remedial
measures
and
sanctions
were
applied.
Also, according to the provisions of the Law on
payment services and electronic money no.
114 of 18.05.2012, the NBM performed ex
ofﬁcio supervision of the activity developed by
payment
services
providers,
including
by
continuous
monitoring
of
their
activity,
analysis of quarterly reports submitted to the
NBM,
examination
of
complaints
(claims)
regarding the activity of payment services
providers and of e-money issuers, review of
information published on web pages etc.
During the year 2017, 75.2 million payment
transactions were performed through providers
of non-bank payment services, which is by
1.8% more transactions than in 2016. These
transactions represented a total value of MDL
21.6 billion, that is by 6.4% more than in 2016
(Chart 5.1).
It is worth mentioning that the
overwhelming majority of these payments were
performed by users – individuals.
