5.3
Automated
interbank
payment system
The automated interbank payment system
(AIPS)
consists
of
the
real-time
gross
settlement system (the RTGS system) and the
designated-time net settlement system (the
DNS system). The RTGS system is designed to
process urgent and high value payments, and
the DNS system is designed to process small
value payments.
In 2017, through AIPS, a total volume of 12.9
million transactions worth of MDL 1 229.4
billion were processed,
increasing both in
number and in value by 7.4% and 48.9%,
respectively, compared to the year 2016. The
average
daily31
volume
of
operations
amounted to 50.7 thousand transactions worth
of MDL 4.8 billion. The maximum volume of
payments settled daily in AIPS was reached on
25 April 2017, being settled 112.7 thousand
payments.
From the payments’ structure point of view,
the largest share of these payments was due
to budget credit transfers (49.1% of total
payments), followed by ordinary customer
transfers (49.0%) and other types of payments
(1.9%).
In terms of value,
the payment
structure
presents
another
picture:
the
amount of ordinary customer transfers (13.8%
of the total amount of payments) exceeded
the amount of budget credit transfers (10.9%),
but the largest share (75.3%) was attributed to
transfers made by banks in their own name
and on their own account.
31In 2017 there were 254 workdays.
78


In 2017, the distribution of the number of
payments per AIPS component systems was as
follows: 8.7% for the RTGS system and 91.3%
for the DNS system.
The distribution of the
payment value has another picture, so that by
RTGS 95.4% of the total payment was settled,
and by DNS only 4.6%.
In 2017, on average, daily, through the RTGS
system, 4.4 thousand payments were settled,
amounting to MDL 4.6 billion, and through the
DNS
system
46.3
thousand
payments,
amounting to MDL 223.0 million. The average
value of a settled payment in the RTGS system
amounted to MDL 1 047.2 thousand, and of a
payment settled in the DNS system – to MDL
4.8 thousand.
Comparing the total value settled through the
RTGS system in 2017 and the annual GDP of
the Republic of Moldova (MDL 150 369 million),
it is estimated that in the RTGS system the
annual GDP equivalent is settled in about 33
operational days.
This indicator reveals the
importance of the RTGS system within the
Moldovan ﬁnancial system.
At
the
Eurosystem’s32
level,
the
gross
settlement system in real time – Target2
processes a volume comparable to the annual
GDP of the euro area every six workdays.
Table A.21 shows the evolution of payments
settled by AIPS compared to 2016.
At the end of 2017, there were registered 16
participants in AIPS, and namely:
• The National Bank of Moldova;
• 11 banks licensed by the National Bank of
Moldova;
• The cash and settlement centre from
Tiraspol;
• The State Treasury of the Ministry of
Finance,
including
as
a
mandated
participant,
who
does
not
have
an
account opened in the AIPS;
• The National Securities Depository of
Moldova.
AIPS supervision
AIPS supervision is conducted to ensure stable
and efﬁcient operation of this system. During
2017,
the
availability
of
the
system
for
participants was 99.9% and was within the set
parameters, and major incidents to disrupt
AIPS activity and affect the participants did not
take place.
32The TARGET 2016 annual report, https://www.ecb.
europa.eu/pub/pdf/other/ecb.targetar2016.en.pdf
5.4
State
securities
book-entry system
The state securities book-entry system (BES) is
a
depository
and
settlement
system
for
securities issued by the Ministry of Finance of
the Republic of Moldova on behalf of the
Government of the Republic of Moldova and of
securities issued by the National Bank. The
ﬁnal settlement for these issuance operations
is carried out in AIPS through the real-time
gross
settlement
system
(RTGS
system),
according to DvP (Delivery versus Payment)
principle. BES is organized and managed by
the National Bank.
BES supervision is performed to ensure stable
and efﬁcient system operation. During 2017,
BES
availability
for
system
participants
accounted for 100% and incidents affecting
operation of the system and participants did
not take place.
5.5
Cashless payment tools
Cashless
payment
tools
are
essential
components of the national payment system
and
the
National
Bank
conducts
their
supervision in order to ensure efﬁciency and
security of their utilization.
Out of payment tools available, the most used
by population (individuals) are payment cards,
the other payment instruments being at the
incipient stage of utilization.
It is worth
mentioning
that
the
NBM
is
undertaking
relevant
measures
together
with
other
international authorities and institutions in
order
to
promote
utilization
of
cashless
payment tools and ﬁght tax evasion.
Payment cards
The number of cards 33 in circulation at the
end of 2017 accounted for 1 736 056 units and
increased by 14.8% compared to the end of
2016, reaching the highest level so far (Chart
5.2).
33Standardized and, where appropriate, customized
information support through which the holder usually
has access to his or her identiﬁcation number and/or
identiﬁcation codes depending on the type of payment
card to the payment account to which the payment card is
attached for the purpose of making payment transactions.
79


Against the backdrop of the increase in the
number of cards in circulation, the number
of active34 cards had approximately the same
growth rate (by 11.4% more than the end of
2016) and the share of active cards in the total
number of cards in circulation did not change
signiﬁcantly, accounting for 58.6%. It is worth
mentioning that this rate of use of cards has
remained virtually the same over the last few
years.

registered for non-cash payments, an increase
of 52.6% compared to the previous year, while
cash
withdrawals
increased
by
12.1%
compared to 2016 (Chart 5.3).

During the year 2017, the following positive
trends emerged on the Moldovan banking cards
market:
• Non-cash payments made with cards
issued
in
the
Republic
of

to

at

the Republic of Moldova developed during

and simplicity of their use, more and
more users choose to pay for purchases
by means of a cashless payment tool.
81


The core indicator for the assessment of the
situation regarding payment card fraud is the
share of frauds in the total value of transactions
through payment cards issued by local licensed
banks, both domestically and abroad. In the
Republic of Moldova this indicator constituted
0.023% for the year 2017, which is below the
European level (0.039% for 201337).

operations,
compared to 2016, there is an upward trend in
utilization of the electronic credit transfers,
which according to estimations, by the end of
2018, can reach a proportion of about 90% of
total number of credit transfer operations
initiated by banks’ customers.
Direct debiting
During the year 2017, through direct debiting
were performed 86 329 payment operations
in a total amount of MDL 37.5 million. Total
number of users of this instrument of payment
represented 3 068 individuals.
82

continued
its
upward
trend,
growth
rate
(+10.7%, up to MDL 21 036.0 million) being
lower by 0.6 p.p. compared to that registered
in 2016.

The National Bank of Moldova is continuously
monitoring the increase of quality level of the
banknotes in circulation, in accordance with
the procedure established by the NBM. Thus
75.65 million banknotes were processed in the
bank to verify authenticity and quality in 2017.
Following such processing, about 51.94 million
banknotes were classiﬁed as inappropriate for
circulation.
Premises of putting into circulation of 1
leu, 2 lei, 5 lei and 10 lei coins
In order to exercise effectively the functions of
the National Bank of Moldova, which operates
as a single issuer of the national currency, the
National Bank continuously ensures adequate
monetary circulation.
An important role is
optimal establishment of the nominal structure
of the national currency, the proportion of
coins versus banknotes.
Thus, in the second half of 2017, the National
Bank conducted an extensive economic study
on utilization of cash on the territory of the
Republic of Moldova and internationally, and
made
the
decision
to
replace
small
denomination banknotes (1 leu, 5 lei and 10
lei) with coins of the same nominal value, and
introduce a new coin with the nominal value –
of 2 lei.
For this purpose, a range of working meetings
with independent experts, representatives of
local
and
central
authorities,
including
business,
banking
and
transport,
were
organized. In August 2017, the NBM initiated a
public consultation process to develop the
85


prototype design of new coins of 1 leu and 2
leu. The public contest was attended by 33
citizens who submitted a total of 80 bids of the
concept for the new metal coins. Inspired by
the sketches proposed by the winners of the
contest,
the
National
Bank
has
further
developed the design of the new metal coins.
6.3
National
currency
counterfeiting
During 2017, the number of counterfeits of
national
currency
in
the
banking
system
totalled 3 960 banknotes, about 39.1% less
than in 2016. As a fraction of the number of
genuine banknotes in circulation at the end of
the reporting period, which constituted 272
million
banknotes,
the
number
of
forged
banknotes
was
at
a
very
low
level
and
represented
0.0015%
or
about
15
fake
banknotes per 1 million genuine banknotes in
circulation.
The most frequently forged banknote was that
of 100 lei – 3 418 counterfeits. In second place
was the banknote with the denomination of
20 lei, with 242 counterfeits, followed by the
200 lei banknote with 192 counterfeits, the
50 lei banknote, with 61 counterfeits and the
1 000 lei banknote, with a number of 29 fakes.
As a basis of comparison, in the year 2016,
the number of counterfeits of national currency
amounted to 6 498 banknotes, and the highest
number of counterfeits were recorded for the
100 lei banknotes, totalling 5 894 banknotes,
20 lei – 376 banknotes, 50 lei – 112 banknotes,
1 000 lei – 66 banknotes.
The share of counterfeits by nominal value in
the years 2017 and 2016 is shown in the chart
6.10 and in the chart 6.11, respectively.
In order to ﬁght counterfeiting of national
currency, the National Bank of Moldova further
recommends the public become familiar with
the safety features of the banknotes on the
NBM website. Also, to be vigilant for possibility
of fraud and to verify the authenticity of
banknotes and coins. In cases that such fraud
takes place, withdraw the banknotes from
circulation and communicate such cases to the
Ministry of Internal Affairs.
At the same time, the NBM, in co-operation
with the Ministry of Internal Affairs, organizes
specialized trainings for licensed banks on
detection and extraction of counterfeits from
circulation.

Chapter 7
Activity related to modiﬁcation of the legal and regulatory
framework
7.1
Prudential
regulation,
regulation
of
accounting evidence in
banks
I. During 2017,
the regulatory framework
related to corporate governance, quality of
shareholders
as
well
as
qualiﬁcation
requirements
set
for
administrators
was
further improved. This process took place in
accordance with the amendments made to the
legal framework regulating banking activity.
In
this
regard,
the
Regulation
on
banks’
activity
management
framework
was
approved.
The
new
provisions
of
the
Regulation
refer
to
corporate
governance
structure, internal control mechanism and risk
management practices in banks.
Based on
these
guidelines,
banks
are
required
to
operate efﬁciently and transparently, to have
an adequate organizational structure, to have
a regulatory framework that corresponds to
the bank’s business model, to manage all the
risks resulting from its activity and to maintain
ﬁnancial stability,
in order to protect the
interests of its depositors.
Also, the banks
have to report cases of fraud that could affect
their safety, soundness and reputation. At the
same time, the banks have to adapt their
policy for appointing administrators to the size
and
complexity
of
their
institution
and
implement a remuneration policy that would
not favour excessive risk-taking.
The
new
provisions
of
Regulation
of
requirements to bank administrators aimed at
enhancing corporate governance by improving
the system of continuous promotion to the
position of administrators of banks persons
who correspond to the ﬁt and proper principle,
and respectively,
at ensuring an efﬁcient
management of the bank.
The Regulation
contains a number of new provisions, based on
the recommendations of the European Banking
Authority Guide and of International Monetary
Fund’s experts, which are intended to outline
the process of appointing and conﬁrming
administrators.
This way,
the banks are
primarily
responsible
for
selecting
and
proposing to the position of an administrator
those candidates who meet the requirements
stipulated in the Regulation as well as for the
re-evaluation of their qualiﬁcations.
The
qualiﬁcations
and
experience
criteria,
established for candidates who were proposed
to the position of an administrator,
were
reviewed
as
well.
Evaluation
for
each
candidate
is
completed
by
applying
the
rational
based
on
signiﬁcant
information
related to the evaluation criteria and taking
into
account
any
other
relevant
aspect,
including the size of the bank, the nature and
complexity of its activities.
Modiﬁcations to the Regulation on holding
participation quotas in the bank’s share capital
aimed to improve the regulatory framework by
establishing mechanisms to allow access to
the banking market only to individuals who
meet
the
legal
requirements
related
to
banking shareholding quality, and namely:
• extension of requirements regarding the
procedure
of
obtaining
prior
written
permission for persons who expect to
receive
shares
of
the
bank
as
a
contribution to their share capital;
• assessment of the quality of potential
acquirer
/
acquirer
by
cumulative
examination and of the criteria related to
excessive stratiﬁcation of shareholding;
• improvement of the set of documents
that follows to be presented by the
potential acquirer / acquirer for its further
examination in the process of granting
prior permission for the solicitation of
holding the quota in the banks’ share
capital;
• obligation of banks to identify and be
aware of all changes in the structure of
their shareholding, including the ultimate
beneﬁcial owner,
and to provide this
information to the supervisory authority.
At the same time, the process of granting
permissions to hold quotas in banks’ share
87


capital was improved for circumstances when
the
potential
acquirer
is
an
international
organization, a multilateral development bank,
a Moldovan bank or a foreign bank.
In this
case, a smaller set of documents is required to
be provided by the above-mentioned entities.
This
process
was
simpliﬁed
for
potential
investors because of their status and the fact
that
the
respective
banks
are
already
supervised by competent authorities.
In order to create the regulatory framework for
setting up branches of foreign banks on the
territory of the Republic of Moldova,
the
Regulation on licensing banks by establishing
the mechanism of setting up a branch of the
foreign bank, was adopted, which includes
licensing requirements, conditions of activity
development, as well as capital requirements.
Therefore, in order to ensure fair and equal
competitive treatment for all participants in
the banking market, the branch of a foreign
bank must have an endowment capital similar
to the capital that a bank shall maintain.
In
this
context,
the
Regulation
on
risk-weighted
capital
adequacy
was
also
adjusted. Respectively, as a component to the
total
regulatory
capital
was
included
the
endowment capital. At the same time, from
the point of view of consolidation of capital by
banks
in
conditions
of
maintaining
their
ﬁnancial
stability,
the
regulation
was
supplemented with provisions, which condition
capital distribution by banks in view of the
impact of economic and social events on
prudential indicators of the bank, determined
inclusively by evaluation of stress tests’ results
performed by the bank and/or by the National
Bank of Moldova.
In order to update and improve the accounting
records
of
licensed
banks,
the
Chart
of
accounts of bookkeeping within licensed banks
of the Republic of Moldova was modiﬁed. The
adjustments were conditioned by entry into
force on January 1, 2018 of the International
Financial
Reporting
Standard
9
“Financial
Tools”, which replaces IAS 39 “Financial tools”
and by amendments to legal and regulatory
acts.
II.
For
the
purpose
of
implementing
the
provisions
of
the
Association
Agreement
signed between the Republic of Moldova and
the European Union on 27.06.2014, which
establishes the commitment of the Republic of
Moldova to comply with the European norms,
including with the European ﬁnancial and
banking directives, and in order to accomplish
the actions stipulated in the National Plan of
Actions
for
implementing
the
Association
Agreement between the Republic of Moldova
and
the
European
Union
in
the
period
2017-2019 (approved by the Government
Decision
no.
1472
of
30.12.2016),
the
Parliament of the Republic of Moldova adopted
the Law on banking activity no. 202 of October
6, 2017.
The new banking law is aimed at improving
national
banking
legislation
taking
into
account
best
international
standards
and
practices related to licensing, regulation and
supervision process of the National Bank of
Moldova. Thus, the provisions of the Directive
2013/36 / EU of 26 June 2013 on the access to
the activity of credit institutions and prudential
supervision
of
credit
institutions
and
of
investment
ﬁrms
and
of
the
Regulation
575/2013 of 26 June 2013 on prudential
requirements
for
credit
institutions
and
investment ﬁrms and on modiﬁcation of the
Regulation (EU) no. 648/2012 were transposed
into the Law.
Therefore,
Basel III standards referring to
capital requirements were transposed into the
Law and, at the same time as the regulation at
the level of legal acts of prudential treatment
of various risks (credit, market, operational,
etc.)
for
calculation
of
risk-weighted
exposures.
In order to create adequate capital reserves
designated to reduce the pro-cyclical nature of
crediting,
to
absorb
the
negative
consequences of excessive crediting or of
economic
recessions
that
cause
ﬁnancial
instability and which have the ultimate goal of
preventing and mitigating macro-prudential
and/or systemic risk,
the Law establishes
additional own funds requirements that the
banks shall hold in order to constitute capital
buffers imposed by the National Bank of
Moldova (capital conservation buffer, bank
speciﬁc countercyclical capital buffer, O-SII
buffer, systemic risk buffer, combined buffer).
Taking into account the provisions of the
Moldova-EU
Association
Agreement,
the
notions of “branch” and “subsidiary” (notions
reversed as meaning in utilization in the
Republic of Moldova compared to international
practices) have been modiﬁed at the Law level.
At the same time, in order to ensure efﬁcient
performance of the supervisor’s competencies
and strengthening corporate governance in
banks, the Law stipulates certain derogations
from the Law on joint stock companies, in
particular regarding attributions of the general
meeting of shareholders, of the council and of
88


the executive body, as well as the exclusion of
the commission of censors in the case of
banks from the Republic of Moldova from the
list of banks’ control bodies.
Compared to the legislation in force at the time
of the approval, the Law includes a wide range
of rights and attributions for the National Bank
of
Moldova
regarding
the
process
of
assessment and supervision of banks, and
continuously promotes the idea of signiﬁcant
increase
and
diversiﬁcation
of
applicable
sanctions and of sanctioning measures.
In
particular, the National Bank of Moldova has
the power to apply pecuniary administrative
sanctions which are sufﬁciently big to have a
deterrent
effect
on
certain
signiﬁcant
deﬁciencies or which pose increased risks.
In
addition
to
the
general
prudential
requirements applicable to the entire banking
sector, the Law includes the requirement for
banks
to
possess
internal
capital
that,
depending on the risks to which they are or
may be exposed, would be appropriate as
distribution, quality and quantity. Banks will
also apply strategies and processes to assess
and maintain the adequacy of their internal
capital.
These rules are part of the second
pillar of Basel III standards.
The basic principles included by the Law refer
to the following:
• creation of a coherent and consolidated
primary
and
secondary
legislative
framework related to banking activity in
the
Republic
of
Moldova,
allowing
effective supervision of banks in the
Republic of Moldova;
• development of certain supervisory tools
necessary
to
prevent
and
minimize
potential shortcomings in the activity of
banks at incipient stages, thus ensuring
stability
of
the
banking
sector
and
protection of depositors’ interests;
• harmonization
of
national
banking
legislation with internationally accepted
standards and principles, and increase of
collaboration relations with supervisory
authorities from other countries to ensure
prudential supervision on a consolidated
basis
and
exchange
of
information,
especially in case of domestic banks,
which are part of international banking
groups.
In order to improve the legal framework of
prudential supervision, taking into account the
commitments of the Republic of Moldova in
the context of the Association Agreement with
the EU of transposing the Directive 2002/87 /
EC of the European Parliament and of the
Council
of
16
December
2002
and
the
developments
on
international
ﬁnancial
markets which determined the emergence of
certain ﬁnancial groups providing services and
products related to different ﬁnancial sectors
called ﬁnancial conglomerates, the Law no.
250 of 1 December 2017 on supplementary
supervision of banks, of insurers / reinsurers
and
investment
ﬁrms
in
a
ﬁnancial
conglomerate was developed and approved.
Taking
into
account
the
fact
that
conglomerates represent the largest ﬁnancial
groups that carry out inter-sectoral activities,
the
Law
stipulates
additional
prudential
supervision at the level of the group in order to
prevent potential contamination risk. Thus, in
order to avoid negative repercussions to the
ﬁnancial sector, to protect the interests of
depositors, insurers and investors in the event
of ﬁnancial instability at conglomerate level,
the Law stipulates consolidated prudential
supervision of banks, of insurers /reinsurers
and of investment ﬁrms which belong to a
ﬁnancial
conglomerate,
in
particular
with
regard
to
solvency,
risk
concentration,
intra-group
transactions,
internal
control
mechanism, internal risk management at the
conglomerate
level,
and
reputation
and
competence of ﬁnancial groups’ management.
The Law establishes measures to facilitate
supplementary
supervision
of
ﬁnancial
conglomerates, the criteria for designating of a
competent authority-coordinator and its tasks,
as well as the provisions on cooperation and
exchange of information between competent
authorities involved, which should carry out
exchange of information, consult each other
before making important decisions and handle
properly
the
claims
for
veriﬁcation
of
information.
The
Law
also
stipulates
requirements
for
mixed ﬁnancial holding companies, regulation
of remedial measures and sanctions, as well as
provisions on supplementary supervision in
case of groups, parent companies of which are
based in other countries - cooperation with
competent authorities from other countries
based on cooperation agreements.
89


7.2
Foreign
Exchange
Operations Regulation
In 2017, the National Bank of Moldova (NBM)
promoted a range of amendments to the legal
framework
related
to
foreign
exchange
regulation. This framework regulates inﬂows
and outﬂows of cash into/from the Republic of
Moldova by banks, the process of submitting
documents for performing payments/transfers
in foreign exchange transactions, notiﬁcation
to
the
NBM
by
residents
about
foreign
loans/credits within operations related to direct
investments, reporting by banks about foreign
exchange operations to the NBM.
I. In order to grant certain new facilities to
banks related to cash transfers into/from the
Republic
of
Moldova,
there
are
foreign
companies, which provide services related to
cash transfers from / to the Republic of
Moldova
and
which
can
offer
more
advantageous
conditions
for
performing
respective operations. In 2017, the Instruction
on introduction/withdrawal of cash into / from
the
Republic
of
Moldova
by
banks
was
modiﬁed.
For
this
purpose,
according
to
the
amendments made, resident banks have been
allowed to perform cash transfers into/out of
the
Republic
of
Moldova
and
based
on
contractual relations with non-resident legal
entities, other than non-resident banks, which
foresee
sale
/
purchase
of
cash
against
cashless ﬁnancial means. At the same time, in
order to minimize eventual risks in relation to
specialized non-resident legal entities which,
compared to banks, may not be subject to
regulation and supervision according to the
procedures established for banks,
certain
criteria
have
been
determined
which
non-resident specialized legal entities shall
meet on a permanent and cumulative basis. In
particular, the specialized legal entity shall not
be registered in an offshore country /area, be
subject to regulation and supervision in the
context of legislation on the prevention and
combating of money laundering and terrorist
ﬁnancing
in
the
country
in
which
it
is
registered, shall be operating in the respective
domain for at least 5 years.
II. To stimulate IT development and in order to
simplify certain requirements for presentation
of documents related to making payments and
transfers in the framework of foreign exchange
operations, certain modiﬁcations have been
made to the Regulation on conditions and
procedure of performing foreign exchange
operations.
These modiﬁcations allow each
resident
payment
services
provider
(RPS
provider) to regulate independently these
aspects according to the speciﬁcs of their
activity.
The principal amendments to the mentioned
regulation refer to the following aspects:
• lighter
requirements
have
been
established for clients’ applications to
perform payments and transfers; the RPS
providers were granted the possibility to
determine on their own the procedure of
documents’
presentation
in
order
to
perform
payments/transfers
within
foreign
exchange
operations,
if
the
normative acts do not stipulate otherwise;
• conditions were established according to
which an individual does not need to
present the identity card to the RPS
provider, in case the RPS provider has not
established stricter rules, the conditions
are meant to be met cumulatively as
follows:
(i)
the
legislation
on
prevention
and
combating
money
laundering
and
terrorist ﬁnancing and/or on providing
payment
services
and
issuance
of
electronic money does not establish the
requirement to identify the individual and
(ii) payment/transfer shall be made by
using the account of the natural person
opened at the RPS provider;
or the
payment/transfer
is
made
without
utilization of the account of the individual
opened at the provider RPS and the
regulation
stipulates
performing
the
payment/transfer without presentation of
justifying documents.
In addition, legal entities have been allowed to
make single payments/transfers in amounts
not exceeding EUR 1 000 without presentation
of justifying documents. The purpose of this
liberalization is to simplify cashless payments /
transfers in insigniﬁcant amounts by legal
persons,
but
the
rule
will
not
apply
to
payments / transfers of resident legal entities
made in the framework of foreign exchange
transactions
subject
to
notiﬁcation
/
authorization in accordance with the Law on
foreign exchange regulation.
III. In order to specify certain aspects related to
notiﬁcation by residents to the NBM about
external loans/credits in the framework of
90


direct investments operations (norms entered
into force on 10.12.2016 according to the Law
no.94 of 13.05.2016 on modiﬁcation and
completion of the Law on foreign exchange
regulation no. 62-XVI of 21 March 2008), the
corresponding amendments to the Instruction
on external commitments were applied in
2017. For this purpose, the conditions were
expressly stipulated under which a loan /
credit under direct investment operations
contracted by a resident from a non-resident
before the date of 10.12.2016, but subject to
modiﬁcations after this date, becomes subject
to notiﬁcation and reporting to the NBM. These
conditions cumulatively to be met are as
follows:
a) the contracting parties and/or the term of the
loan/credit and/or the contract amount and/or
the share held by the creditor in the debtor’s
capital have modiﬁed, constituting less than
10%;
b) it ﬁts into the loan/credit speciﬁed in item
2.5 letter (a), (b) or (d) of the instruction;
c) the amount of the loan/credit exceeds EUR
50 000 (or its equivalent).
At
the
same
time,
the
aforementioned
instruction was supplemented with a new
provision stipulating that taking into account
or authorizing external commitments by the
NBM
does
not
exempt
resident
payment
services providers, through which notiﬁed /
authorized foreign exchange operations are
performed,
from
complying
with
other
provisions of the legislation of the Republic of
Moldova, including the provisions related to
enforcement of measures in accordance with
the requirements of legislation in the domain
of
prevention
and
combating
money
laundering and terrorism ﬁnancing.
IV.
The
need
of
adjusting
the
reporting
framework for foreign exchange operations
has conditioned in 2017 the modiﬁcation and
completion of the Instruction on reporting
certain
foreign
exchange
operations
by
licensed banks. In this regard, the following
reports were modiﬁed:
a) the report “Cash imported into/exported
from the Republic of Moldova by banks”.
The modiﬁcation of this report was linked to
the Decision of the Executive Board of the
NBM no.35 of 8 February 2017 “On the
modiﬁcation and completion of the Instruction
on introduction/exportation of cash into/from
the Republic of Moldova by banks”, by which
the
licensed
banks
were
allowed
to
import/export cash into/from the Republic of
Moldova not only on the basis of contractual
relations with non-resident banks but also on
the
basis
of
contractual
relations
with
specialized non-resident legal entities;
b) the report “Operations of buying and selling
foreign currency performed by a licensed
bank”;
c) the report “Licensed bank’s operations with
cash in foreign currency and with traveller’s
checks in foreign currency”.
The amendment of these reports was intended
to specify a few aspects regarding the manner
of reﬂecting foreign exchange transactions in
cash with individuals (the number of operations
and their amount), in case that during the
reporting period revocation by individuals of
the operations concerned took place.
91


Chapter 8
External activity of the NBM
8.1
International
Collaboration
of
the
Republic of Moldova
International Monetary Fund (IMF)
The
International
Monetary
Fund
is
an
international
ﬁnancial
institution
with
the
fundamental mission of ensuring stability of
the international monetary system. In order to
accomplish this mission, the IMF is directing its
activity
to
monitoring
the
economic
and
ﬁnancial policies of member states, promoting
international
monetary
cooperation
and
currency stability, granting temporary ﬁnancial
assistance
to
countries
with
balance
of
payments disequilibrium, and grants technical
assistance to IMF member countries.
The
Republic
of
Moldova
is
member
of
the
International Monetary Fund as of August 12,
1992.
Current quota of the Republic of Moldova at IMF
amounts to 172.5 million of special drawing
rights (SDRs), which represents 0.04 percent
of the IMF’s capital. This quota entered into
force on the 17th of February 2016, following
the increase by the Republic of Moldova of its
previous quota of (123.2 million SDRs) by 49.3
million SDR. The voting power of our country
at the IMF is of 1,973 votes, which represents
0.08% of the total number of votes.
Throughout 2017, the Republic of Moldova
advanced its collaboration relations with the
IMF, based on a complex program of economic
reforms and ﬁnancial support,
which was
approved on November 7,2016 by the IMF
Executive Board for a period of three years.
The Program is implemented through two
lending tools - the Extended Fund Facility39
(EFF) and the Extended Credit Facility40 (ECF),
39EFF which stipulates an annual interest rate equal to
the SDRs’ core interest rate, a 10-years reimbursement
term and a grace period of four and a half years.
40(ECF) which stipulates an interest rate equal to zero by
the end of the year 2018, a grace period of ﬁve and a half
years and a repayment term of 10 years. Two thirds of the
loan amount is granted on ECF basis.
totalling 129.4 million SDRs (is equivalent to
about USD 183.1 million and to 75% of the
Republic of Moldova quota held at the IMF).
According to the terms of the program, the
disbursement
of
funds
takes
place
in
6
instalments, preceded by an assessment of
the conditions for each instalment.
The ﬁrst instalment amounting to SDRs 26
million (about USD 35.9 million) was disbursed
to the authorities of the Republic of Moldova
under the IMF Program in November 2016,
immediately
after
the
Program
has
been
approved, and during the year 2017 another
two
separate
instalments
were
disbursed
totalling SDRs 31.4 million (about USD 43.7
million). The remaining amount follows to be
disbursed
during
the
next
stages
of
the
implementation of the program.
The main objective of the program supported
by the IMF in 2017 was focused on reforms in
the ﬁnancial and banking sector,
prudent
management
of
public
ﬁnances
and
improvement of the operational framework in
the energy sector. In the banking domain, the
program aimed on strengthening governance
and
of
ﬁnancial
situation
of
banks
by
increasing
transparency
of
shareholders,
compliance of owners and administrators of
banks
with
the
criteria
of
honesty
and
professionalism,
identiﬁcation
of
related
parties lending, and ensuring compliance with
regulatory and capital requirements.
In order to organize regular consultations and
perform assessments related to the level of
implementation of the commitments assumed
by the IMF Program, during the year 2017, IMF
experts performed 3 working visits to Chisinau
(14-28 February, 17-21 July and 25 October –
07 November).
As a result of the visits,
ﬁnalization of the two rounds of assessments
of the Program with the IMF was possible as
well
as
the
development
of
regular
consultations
for
the
year
2017
in
the
framework of Article IV of the IMF Statute41.
According to ﬁndings of IMF experts, as a result
of the visits, the Republic of Moldova had a
41Article IV of the IMF Statute governs the cooperation
relations between the IMF and the member countries.
Pursuant to the provisions of this article, annually the IMF
grants consultations to the Republic of Moldova in the
economic and ﬁnancial policy domain
92


relative macroeconomic and ﬁnancial stability
in 2017, overcoming the banking crisis of 2014.
Signiﬁcant progress has been made by the
Moldovan
authorities
in
eliminating
the
long-term
persistent
vulnerabilities
of
the
ﬁnancial-banking sector and in advancing the
implementation of structural reforms.
The
efforts made during the year signiﬁcantly
contributed to strengthening the ﬁnancial
stability and to relaunching the economy.
In addition,
IMF experts ascertained that
signiﬁcant progress has been made in 2017 in
terms
of
the
ﬁnancial
-
banking
sector
recovery,
including
strengthening
the
supervisory
and
regulatory
framework,
improving
management
and
increasing
transparency of shareholders.
Strengthening the cooperation relations of the
National Bank of Moldova with the IMF was
possible due to the visits made to Washington
D. C. (October 10-15 and April 17-23) by the
NBM leadership, in the context of participation
in annual and spring meetings of the IMF and
of the World Bank Group.
During the visits, members of the Executive
Board of the NBM had a series of meetings
with ofﬁcials of the IMF, the World Bank,
European Commission, EBRD Treasury USA,
the International Finance Corporation,
the
Council of Europe Development Bank and
other
important
partners
to
discuss
the
implementation of the Program with the IMF,
the achievements and priorities in the process
of reforms in the ﬁnancial-banking sector, the
investment framework in the Republic of
Moldova and measures taken in 2017 to
ensure macroeconomic stability of the country.
In addition, the successes achieved by the
authorities of the Republic of Moldova in
implementation of the Program with the IMF
were
discussed
by
the
Governor
of
the
National Bank during the bilateral meeting
with the IMF Managing Director, in the context
of participation at the high level conference
Reaccelerating Convergence in Central and
South-eastern Europe, organized by the IMF on
July 10-11, 2017 in Dubrovnik. In this context,
the IMF leadership welcomed the successes
achieved by the Moldovan authorities during
2017 and
emphasized the
importance of
continuing the reform implementation agenda.
The meeting of the Moldovan delegation with
the managing Director of the IMF can be
considered a strong sign of support and
credibility grunted to our country on behalf of
development partners.
In
the
context
of
ﬁnancial
arrangements,
during the year 2017, the National Bank of
Moldova received from the IMF 12.4 million
SDRs (USD 17.26 million) offered through the
EFF ﬁnancing mechanism to strengthen the
international
reserves
of
the
Republic
of
Moldova. Table A.23 reﬂects the cumulative
ﬁnancial arrangements that the National Bank
of Moldova beneﬁted from by the end of 2017.
In the reporting year,
total payments for
servicing loans granted to the National Bank of
Moldova by the IMF amounted to SDRs 46.25
million (the equivalent of USD 64 million), of
which payments for the principal loan amount
constituted SDRs 44.46 million (the equivalent
of USD 61.52 million) and payments for loans
interests amounted to SDRs 1.79 million (the
equivalent of USD 2.48 million).
European Union (EU)
In 2017, the Republic of Moldova advanced on
its way of gradual accession to the European
Union in the context of implementation of
provisions
of
the
Association
Agreement
signed in Brussels on the 27 June 2014
between the Republic of Moldova on one side
and the European Union and the European
Atomic Energy Community and their member
countries, on the other side.
In
order
to
ensure
continuity
in
the
implementation of the Association Agreement,
the Republic of Moldova and the European
Union agreed on a new Association Agenda for
the period 2017-2019, which was published in
the Ofﬁcial Journal of the EU on the 19th of
August
2017
and
which
replaced
the
Association
Agenda
2014
-2016,
initially
agreed on the 26 of June 2014.
The new
document has the goal of supporting and
strengthening the resilience and stability of the
Republic of Moldova, while pursuing a closer
political association and a deeper economic
integration. The 2017-2019 Agenda foresees a
list
of
short
and
medium-term
priorities,
including in the areas, which target the tasks
of the NBM, such as: ﬁnancial services, current
payments
and
capital
ﬂow,
right
of
establishment and trade in services, economic
development and market opportunities.
In order to monitor the European integration
process at the national level, the Moldovan
authorities have progressed on their way of
implementing the provisions of the National
Action Plan of the Republic of Moldova - EU
Association Agreement (NAPAA) for the years
2017-2019, approved by the Decision of the
93


Government of the Republic of Moldova no.
1472 from December 30, 2016. This document
was
developed
based
on
the
proposals
received
from
relevant
public
institutions,
including
from
the
NBM,
containing
key
cooperation
priorities
in
fulﬁlling
the
commitments stipulated in the Association
Agreement with the European Union.
Therefore, the National Bank of Moldova is
committed to implement the Plan through a
range of actions,
such as:
alignment of
legislation on the central bank to the EU’s best
practices, strengthening the NBM’s capacity in
the ﬁeld of banking regulation and supervision,
development
and
strengthening
of
requirements
related
to
governance
and
banking risk management, transposition and
implementation of EU legislation in the ﬁeld of
ﬁnancial-banking services, etc.
The main role in coordination and monitoring
at
the
national
level
of
the
European
integration process during 2017 belonged to
the Governmental Commission for European
Integration, a member of which is the NBM
governor.
During
the
meetings
of
the
Governmental
Commission
for
European
Integration,
held
in
2017,
there
were
discussed,
among
other
issues,
the
developments of the NAPAA implementation in
the period 2014-2016 and the priorities of the
new NAPAA for the period 2017-2019, the
implementation of the Association Agenda RM -
EU for the period 2017-2019, the Deep and
Comprehensive Free Trade Agreement (DCFTA)
and preparation for regular meetings with the
European partners.
Thus, in the context of cooperation between
the Republic of Moldova and the European
Union, during 2017, the representatives of the
National Bank of Moldova participated in
meetings
of
institutional
cooperation
organizations established by the Association
Agreement:
the
Association
Council,
the
Association
Committee,
the
Association
Committee
in
Trade
Conﬁguration,
Subcommittee on economic and other sectors
cooperation (cluster I).
During these meetings a range of issues were
discussed referring to the evolution of certain
macroeconomic
indicators,
the
current
situation in the area of maintaining ﬁnancial
stability and of banking system consolidation,
as
well
as
in
banking
regulation
and
supervision domain, the progress related to
the area of banking sector reforms, measures
of
fraudulent
practices
prevention
in
the
banking
sector,
progress
in
Association
Agreement implementation, etc.
The relations of the Republic of Moldova with
the European partners were marked in 2017
by signing in Brussels, on 23 of November, of
the
package
of
documents
on
granting
macro-ﬁnancial assistance to the Republic of
Moldova
by
the
European
Union.
The
Memorandum of Understanding was signed by
the European Commissioner for economic and
ﬁnancial affairs, taxation and customs, by the
governor of the National Bank of Moldova and
by the minister of Finance in the presence of
the prime minister.
The Memorandum of Understanding stipulates
the disbursement of EUR 100 million, in three
instalments, in accordance with the schedule
of implementation of 28 reforms and actions
by Chisinau authorities.
Among them were
included
a
range
of
actions
in
the
ﬁnancial-banking ﬁeld, such as the approval of
the regulatory framework for transposition of
European
directives
and
of
international
principles in the area of prudential supervision,
transparency
of
banks’
shareholders,
improvement
of
the
deposits
guarantee
system, etc.
During the visit of the Moldovan delegation to
Brussels
on
the
occasion
of
signing
the
Memorandum, besides the meetings with the
European Commissioner and the European
Commissioner for Neighbourhood Policy and
Enlargement Negotiations, the NBM governor
also had working meetings with the Deputy
Director-General for EU Neighbourhood Policy
and Enlargement Negotiations, the Director for
International
Economic
Relations
and
the
Deputy
Director-General
of
the
European
External Action Service for Russia, Eastern
Partnership, Central Asia and the OSCE. During
the meetings, the governor of the NBM spoke
about the progress in implementation of the
IMF
program
and
presented
information
received from Kroll and Steptoe &Johnson on
ﬁnalization of the investigation report and on
transparency of the report’s conclusions.
Another major event that marked the dialogue
of the NBM with the EU in 2017 is the
successful ﬁnalization of the Twinning Project
entitled Strengthening the National Bank of
Moldova’s capacity in the ﬁeld of banking
regulation and supervision in the context of EU
requirements funded by the European Union.
The project was launched on 30th of June 2015
and implemented during two years by the
National Bank of Moldova together with the
consortium of the National Bank of Romania
94


and the Central Bank of Netherlands.
The
ofﬁcial closing events of the project were held
in
Bucharest
(16.05.2017)
and
Chisinau
(30.05.2017), bringing together senior ofﬁcials,
ambassadors of the European Union, ofﬁcials
of the National Bank of Moldova and of the
National Bank of Romania, and of the banking
community and experts.
One of the key priorities of the NBM, in line
with the Association Agreement concluded
between the Republic of Moldova and the
European Union (EU) signed on June 27, 2014,
is close cooperation with the central banks of
the EU. Thus, during the year 2017, the NBM
advanced on the way of establishing new
cooperation partnerships with other central
banks of the European Union, in order to gain
experience in various areas that fall within its
competence.
Group
of
Banking
Supervisors
from
Central and Eastern Europe (BSCEE)
In 2017, the National Bank of Moldova, as a
member of the Group of Banking Supervisors
in the Central and Eastern Europe, continued
to work with BSCEE members by exchanging
information and experience in the ﬁeld of
banking supervision and regulation.
Thus,
between 31 May and 2 June 2017, an annual
conference on the implementation of the
existing legal framework in the European
Union on Basel III was organized by the Group
of Banking Supervisors of Central and Eastern
Europe
(BSCEE).
The
participation
of
the
National Bank of Moldova in this event took
place in the context of achieving the strategic
objectives of the National Bank of Moldova,
and speciﬁcally the transposition into the
national legislation of the CRD IV package of
the
EU
(Capital
Requirements
Directive)
through the Twinning project.
It is worth noting that representatives of the
National Bank of Moldova participate regularly
in the events organized by BSCEE, which offer
the
possibility
to
communicate
with
the
regulatory and supervisory authorities of other
states. Taking advantage of this opportunity,
during 2017, the National Bank of Moldova
collaborated with BSCEE members on the
subject of compliance risk regulation.
Cooperation
with
other
central
banks
and
banking
supervisory
authorities
from other countries
In
the
context
of
strengthening
the
cooperation
relations
with
the
Bank
of
Lithuania, on 22.08.2017, the Memorandum of
Understanding between the National Bank of
Moldova and the Bank of Lithuania (Lietuvos
bankas) was signed during a working visit of
the NBM delegation to Vilnius. The document
signed by the Governor of the NBM and the
Governor of the Bank of Lithuania stipulates
the
establishment
of
a
comprehensive
framework of cooperation and exchange of
information between the institutions.
The
signed Memorandum offers new opportunities
to extend technical assistance in the banking
sector and to attract expertise of the EU
central banks.
At
the
same
time,
in
the
framework
of
exercising the banking licensing function, in
2017,
the
National
Bank
of
Moldova
collaborated
with
banking
supervising
authorities
from
other
states
(Belarus,
Lithuania, Georgia, Russia, Czech Republic,
Ukraine,
Kyrgyzstan,
Italy
and
Romania)
achieving a mutual exchange of information
required in the process of assessment of the
quality of individuals (“Fit & Proper”), which
have developed an authorized and supervised
activity by these authorities.
Black Sea Trade and Development Bank
(BSTDB)
The BSTDB was established on the basis of the
Agreement signed on June 30, 1994 in Tbilisi
by 11 states participating in the Black Sea
Economic Cooperation, including the Republic
of Moldova.
BSTDB aims to ﬁnance various
regional programs speciﬁc for transition to
market economy, and the Republic of Moldova,
one of the founders of the BSTDB, ratiﬁed the
Agreement on the establishment of BSTDB by
Parliament Decision no. 670 from November
28, 1995.
On July 3, 2017, the National Bank of Moldova
signed the Memorandum of Understanding with
the Black Sea Trade and Development Bank, at
the Regional Business Forum emph Moldova –
Working Together to Support Growth, organized
on the occasion of the Annual Meeting of the
Board of Governors of the Black Sea Trade and
Development Bank which in 2017 took place in
Chisinau.
95


The document signed by the Governor of the
NBM and President of the BSTDB stipulates
creation of necessary framework to facilitate
cooperation between the parties and extension
of technical assistance in the banking ﬁeld. At
the same time, the Memorandum offers new
opportunities to attract international expertise
in areas of high interest for the National Bank
of Moldova on behalf of the countries of the
Black Sea region.
External
technical
assistance
and
collaboration
with
international
institutions and central banks of other
countries
In the context of the NBM cooperation with
international
institutions
and
with
central
banks of other countries, during 2017, the
National Bank of Moldova beneﬁted from
technical assistance from a range of external
donors in order to strengthen its capacities in
various
ﬁelds,
such
as
monetary
policy
framework assessment, strengthening banking
regulation
and
supervisory
mechanism,
aligning banking legislation to EU standards,
creation of the Central Securities Depository,
making
external
communication
more
effective, investment tools diversiﬁcation and
other areas speciﬁc for the central bank.
Thus, due to the technical assistance of the
IMF,
assessment
of
the
monetary
policy
framework in the context of achieving the
strategic objective of price stability became
possible. During the visits of IMF experts to
Chisinau
(19.06.2017
-
30.06.2017
and
28-29.09.2017) an extensive exchange of
opinions
took
place
on
a
few
potential
elements of quantitative substantiation tools
for monetary policy decisions, methods of
improving the monetary policy framework and
making NBM external communication more
effective.
Activities related to banking regulation and
supervision remain among the core priorities
of the NBM. Therefore, in order to strengthen
the capacities in these areas, the central bank
beneﬁted
from
technical
assistance
from
several external donors.
In this context, reviewing banks’ policies and
procedures in the area of risk management of
related parties has been an important subject
of discussions during the visits of IMF experts
to Chisinau. The meetings developed on the
occasion of visits gave the possibility to the
NBM employees to get acquainted with the
policies and procedures of related parties’ risk
management,
to
apply
the
identiﬁcation
features of banks’ related parties and revise
the SREP Guide in order to include the speciﬁc
references regarding the related parties’ risks.
The technical assistance granted by the IMF
gave the possibility to share the experience of
the National Bank of Ukraine in developing
presumptions of afﬁliation and implementation
of exposure limits for banks’ related parties.
In the framework of the project EU High Level
Advisers’ Mission to the Republic of Moldova
2016-2018, an EU high-level adviser offered
consultancy during the year 2017 in the area
of NBM policies. Assistance was provided in
the context of banking supervisory reform, in
order
to
create
a
modern
and
efﬁcient
supervisory framework in line with the EU
standards and aimed to strengthen the NBM
communication capacities and cooperation
with
other
institutions
for
effectively
counteracting ﬁnancial crimes.
Also, during the year 2017, the National Bank
of Moldova continued to receive EU assistance
through the Twinning Project for the purpose of
gradual
harmonization
of
prudential
regulations
with
the
European
CRD
IV
framework (Directive no.
36/2013) / CRR
(Regulation no. 575/2013), which incorporates
Basel III rules and standards.
Within the Twinning Project Strengthening the
National Bank of Moldova capacity in the ﬁeld
of banking regulation and supervision domain
in the context of EU requirements, the NBM
beneﬁted from the support provided by experts
of the National Bank of Romania ((NBR) and
of the Central Bank of Netherlands (CBN) in
order to transpose the European regulatory
framework.
The Twinning Project started on
the 30th of June 2015 and was completed on
the 30th of May 2017.
The main results have been achieved due to a
range of activities, among which shall be
remarked training of employees, exchange of
experience
with
foreign
experts,
implementation of impact surveys and of
certain thematic evaluations. In this context,
in collaboration with experts from the NBR, the
impact of introducing CRD IV / CRR liquidity
requirements over the banking sector of the
Republic of Moldova was assessed in terms of
banks’ enrolment within the established limits.
Within the assessment, two liquidity indices of
the Basel III international regulatory framework
were calculated: the liquidity coverage ratio
(Liquidity Coverage Ratio - LCR) and the net
stable funding ratio (Net Stable Funding Ratio -
96


NSFR).
Beneﬁts of the Twinning Project will
become
visible
together
with
the
full
implementation
of
the
new
regulatory
framework
and
the
application
of
new
risk-based supervisory practices. Although the
direct beneﬁciaries of the project are the NBM
and the licensed banks in the Republic of
Moldova, the ﬁnal beneﬁciaries are the citizens
of the Republic of Moldova, who will enjoy a
more viable, stable and secure banking system.
Also, establishment of a new legal framework
equivalent to that applicable in the European
states contributes to improving the image of
the Moldovan banking sector and to attracting
new investors.
Therefore,
the adequate
activity of the banks, especially the intense
process of lending,
stimulates the overall
economic growth in the Republic of Moldova.
In particular, it is worth noting that one of the
major achievements registered as a result of
the Twinning Project is the approval of the Law
on banking activity no. 202 of 06.10.2017, with
the application of provisions of 01.01.2018.
Due to fruitful cooperation relationships with
the Deutsche Bundesbank, the NBM beneﬁted
during the year 2017 from technical assistance
in the ﬁeld of banking supervision, focusing in
particular
on
minimum
requirements
for
banking capital, capital concept under Basel II
/
III
agreement,
capital
adequacy,
risk
management and banks’ recovery plans.
At the same time, sharing the experience of
the German central bank regarding IT tools
utilized both for on-site and off-site supervision
has helped to create a vision of the NBM on
the IT tools used, for determining additional
requirements for the development of the
current Credit Register, as well as of the need
for additional solicitations of information from
the banks for supervisory purposes.
In the context of commitments made by the
Republic of Moldova by signing the Association
Agreement with the European Union,
the
National
Bank
of
Moldova
has
attracted
technical
assistance
from
development
partners in order to monitor transparency of
shareholders of licensed banks. Thus, due to
the technical assistance from experts from
Expert-Grup
and
PricewaterhouseCoopers,
offered through the program Good Governance
Fund, ﬁnanced by the UK Government, the
NBM gained expertise in developing technical
speciﬁcations and of tender documents for the
acquisition of the IT solution for monitoring
shareholders’
transparency
and
remote
analysis
in
the
ﬁeld
of
prevention
and
combating money laundering and terrorist
ﬁnancing.
Throughout 2017, the National Bank of Moldova
continued directing its efforts to achieving the
objective of maintaining ﬁnancial stability of
the Republic of Moldova banking system. For
this purpose, the National Bank of Moldova
beneﬁtted from consultancy services of USA
Treasury experts in strengthening corporate
governance of banks, structural and editorial
aspects of writing the Financial Stability Report,
and in reorganizing the National Committee for
Financial Stability.
In order to strengthen the NBM capacities in
the domain of bank liquidation, during 2017,
USA Treasury experts provided assistance and
consultancy on such issues, as transactions on
sale of assets of banks in liquidation and the
attributions and responsibilities of liquidators.
Taking into account that the Law on banks’
recovery and resolution came into force in
October 2016, the assistance granted in 2017
by
the
USA
Treasury,
the
International
Monetary Fund, the Central Bank of Germany,
and the Romanian Banking Institute in the ﬁeld
of recovery and resolution of banks was
extremely beneﬁcial. Within the established
collaboration a range of issues have been
addressed, such as: drafting resolution plans,
recovery plans, application of resolution tools,
modality of identifying systemic banks based
on the methodology of the Basel Committee
on effective supervision, continuity of critical
functions of the bank, saving the viable assets
of the bank in case of application of the
resolution, as well as application of bankruptcy
liquidation procedures of banks with a few
elements of the resolution.
Additionally, at the solicitation of the National
Bank of Moldova, in September 2017, the
International
Monetary
Fund
granted
consultancy
on
improving
the
regulatory
framework
for
liquidity
assistance
in
emergency situations.
Issues undergoing
counselling referred to the term for which
liquidity assistance is provided in emergency
situations, the criteria for granting assistance,
such as solvency, systemic importance of the
bank,
eligible
collateral
and
evaluation
methodology,
cooperation
and
sharing
responsibilities between relevant authorities in
situations of crisis, as well as the conditions
that banks have to meet before and after
providing liquidity assistance in emergency
situations. Following the recommendations of
the IMF, the current regulatory framework on
granting liquidity assistance in emergency
97


situations by the NBM to banks follows to be
amended in order to reﬂect the best practices
in the ﬁeld.
Starting from the need to implement the new
ﬁnancial reporting standard IFRS 9, as of
January 1, 2018, the National Bank of Moldova
has requested technical assistance from the
World Bank related to classiﬁcation of ﬁnancial
tools
and
estimation
of
expected
losses
associated with credit risk. The assistance was
granted in the period of December 13-14,
2017, during the visit of World Bank experts to
Chisinau, being focused on determining the
most appropriate classiﬁcation of ﬁnancial
assets and on the best solutions for assessing
expected credit risk losses, starting from the
structure and quality of ﬁnancial assets in the
NBM portfolio.
At the same time, familiarization with the Bank
of Lithuania’s asset allocation, investment tools
and risk quantiﬁcation related to them, the
information systems used to estimate, monitor
and report the risks as well as the performance
of managed portfolios was possible thanks to
a documentary visit from 24 to 26 October
2017 to the Central Bank of Lithuania, ﬁnanced
through the TAIEX program42.
In
order
to
strengthen
the
institutional
capacities
of
the
NBM
in
the
ﬁeld
of
preservation
and
management
of
foreign
exchange reserves of the state, the European
Fund for Southeast Europe facilitated the
participation of a representative of the NBM at
the
seminar
Reserves
and
Assets
Management,
organized
at
the
Swiss
Confederation by the Bank for International
Settlements, between September 11 and 15,
2017. The topics of the seminar focused on
asset
allocation
under
current
market
conditions, tactical asset allocation, Renmimbi,
Bitcoin and Blockchain, building a portfolio and
calculating its performance. In the context of
this seminar, BIS provided participants with
access to a module for performing the asset
allocation
exercise
(BAAM
–
BIS
Asset
Management Asset Allocation Module).
In the context of actions to create the Central
Securities Depository (CSD), during the year
2017, the NBM received technical assistance
42TAIEX
is
a
technical
assistance
and
information
exchange tool of the European Commission aimed at
supporting the beneﬁciary authorities in the process of
harmonizing national legislation with the EU legislation.
TAIEX
assistance
is
provided
to
beneﬁciary
public
authorities
for
a
short-term
(up
to
5
days)
in
the
form of workshops, EU expert missions to beneﬁciary
institutions,
or
study
visits
of
representatives
from
beneﬁciary authorities to EU countries’ authorities.
from
experts
of
the
Financial
Services
Volunteer Corps (FSVC) through the United
States Agency for International Development
(USAID).
The
assistance
was
aimed
to
implement a series of activities in order to
facilitate the process of establishing the CSD,
focusing on reaching the level of compliance of
the
newly
created
Central
Securities
Depository
with
international
standards,
including
with
the
ﬁnancial
market
infrastructure principles developed by the
Bank for International Settlements (BIS) and
the International Organization of Securities
Commissions (IOSCO).
The development of an innovative, accessible,
efﬁcient and safe national payment system
remains one of the core priorities of the NBM.
For
this
purpose,
the
NBM
has
received
technical assistance from the FSVC experts to
prepare a report on ﬁnancial inclusion and
feasibility of mobile payments. Foreign experts
assessed the local ﬁnancial market to identify
both
the
potential
beneﬁts
and
risks
of
introducing mobile payments as well as to
formulate recommendations that would help
increase ﬁnancial inclusion based on research
of experience and on existing implementation
patterns in other countries.
In its capacity of supplier of publications and
analytical materials, which include national
statistical data, the NBM beneﬁted during
2017 from technical assistance of the IMF in
preparing
forecasts
of
external
sector’s
indicators.
Also, the NBM representatives in charge of
macroeconomic statistics collaborated with the
National Bank of Serbia (NBS) for organization
of the collecting, storage and data processing
process, as well as for the technical support
needed for compilation and production of
international
account
indicators.
In
this
respect, in the period of July 12-13 2017, NBM
representatives made a documentary visit to
the NBS in order to implement its experience
in these areas.
In particular, we note that the National Bank of
Moldova participated as a member of the
Technical
Working
Group
on
statistics
of
international trade in services, foreign direct
investments and commercial statistics related
to foreign subsidiaries in two sessions under
the Central European Agreement (CEFTA), to
which Moldova is a part as of 2017. The CEFTA
Secretariat has launched the test version of a
data
exchange
system
between
member
countries. In this context, the NBM contributed
with statistics of international accounts of the
98


Republic
of
Moldova
in
completing
this
database.
CEFTA representatives have supported the
efforts of the NBM in developing the statistics
of international trade in services, for this
purpose, a working visit to Chisinau by a
CEFTA expert in the period of April 4 – 7, 2017
was organized. The ﬁnal goal of this visit was
to
conduct
a
general
evaluation
on
the
possibility of expanding the capacity of the
NBM to produce statistics on foreign trade with
services according to CEFTA requirements,
including
the
analysis
of
possibilities
of
expanding
geographical
distribution
of
respective indicators and utilization in the
process of well-known experience in this ﬁeld.
Taking into account that a modern human
resources
management
system
plays
an
important role in the effectiveness of the
organization’s
functioning,
the
NBM
representatives
had
the
opportunity
to
implement the experience of the National
Bank of Romania (NBR) in performing certain
practical
activities
related
to
employees’
recruitment process.
At the same time, the
visit to Bucharest permitted to share the
experience of the NBM in the ﬁeld of strategic
planning,
as well as in organization and
implementation of processes management
system at the NBM.
At the same time, starting from the objective
of strengthening human resources in the
domain of banking regulation and supervision,
during
the
period
19-20.01.2017
and
20
-21.04.2017, the NBM beneﬁtted from technical
assistance from the NBR (within the Twinning
project) on aspects related to identiﬁcation of
employees’
development
needs
in
the
respective ﬁelds.
Additionally, during the
period 01.01.2017 - 27.02.2017, thanks to the
technical assistance from the UK Government,
there was accumulated and utilized knowledge
in the area of human resources management,
by identifying optimization opportunities in
classiﬁcation of job positions, in increasing the
retention of employees,
in reviewing and
standardizing job descriptions, and of the
performance management system.
During 2017, the National Bank of Moldova
also beneﬁted from technical assistance in the
area of external communication,
which is
essential in the process of dissemination of
thematic information and of improving the
image of the institution in front of citizens and
foreign partners. In this regard, the Deutsche
Bundesbank Centre for Technical Central Bank
Cooperation
and
USAID
offered
training
opportunities to the NBM employees within a
range of seminars supported by international
experts.
The seminars were designated to
provide necessary information support for
streamlining
the
external
and
internal
communication processes carried out by the
central bank.
A modern communication
approach, in line with the expertise of other
countries,
will help to optimize the NBM
activities and increase its credibility. The topic
of
external
communication
was
also
the
destination of the documentary visit of the
communication team from the National Bank
of Moldova to the National Bank of Romania,
organized
by
PricewaterhouseCoopers
Romania under the Good Governance Fund
project, ﬁnanced by the Government of Great
Britain. The respective support also included
performing
an
opinions
survey
on
the
perception of the National Bank of Moldova by
public. Subsequently, the results obtained and
the related consultancy were implemented by
ﬁnalizing a communication strategy.
Financial
education
among
population
undoubtedly contributes to increasing and
promoting
transparency,
conﬁdence
and
attractiveness of the banking sector.
The
National
Bank
of
Moldova
is
constantly
participating in the process of promoting
ﬁnancial education at national level alongside
other relevant institutions. In this regard, the
National Bank of Moldova beneﬁtted from the
ﬁnancial and technical assistance provided by
the EFSE (European Fund for Southeastern
Europe) in implementing the International
Week of Financial Education Project.
The
interactive games developed and organized by
the NBM for children of various age groups,
together with other educational activities,
represent
an
important
perspective
of
promoting ﬁnancial inclusion.
In other ways, we note that, in the context of
identifying
modalities
to
manage
excess
liquidity
in
the
domestic
banking
sector,
representatives of the German Economic Team
in Moldova (GET Moldova), working in a project
of the independent consulting centre Berlin
Economics,
funded
by
the
German
Government, have collaborated to develop a
range of recommendations and solutions.
It is to be noted, that GET Moldova provided
assistance to Moldovan authorities, including
to the NBM, by identifying current economic
issues and developing recommendations for
their
solution,
based
on
an
independent
analysis and expertise. Also, on November 9,
2017, at the invitation of GET Moldova and of
the German Council for International Relations
(DGAP), the governor of the NBM made a
99


working visit to Berlin, where he attended a
seminar dedicated to the Republic of Moldova
economic and banking reforms.
During the
event, the NBM governor reported on the
progress of institutional and legislative reforms
in
the
ﬁnancial-banking
domain,
and
in
particular
remarked
the
approval
of
the
banking law which transposes the new banking
supervision principles Basel III, measures of
making
banking
shareholding
more
transparent,
ﬁnancial
consolidation
of
systemic
banks
and
the
main
stages
of
implementation of the program with the IMF. At
the same time, during the working visit, the
governor of the NBM had meetings with
representatives of the Federal Government, of
ﬁnancial institutions and with representatives
of foreign embassies in Berlin.
100


Chapter 9
Internal activity of NBM
9.1
Corporate
Governance
of the National Bank
The Law on the National Bank of Moldova
provides
the
legal
authority
and
the
governance framework of the National Bank.
The
structure
of
corporate
governance
includes
senior
management
bodies,
high-level committees - the Audit Committee
and the Investment Committee - and several
levels of control - external and internal. This
structure is complemented by the compliance
and ethics framework - the Code of Conduct
for NBM Employees, the Anti-Fraud Policy of
the National Bank of Moldova, the Information
Security Policy, as well as public access to the
NBM
normative
acts,
statistics
and
publications.
Governing bodies of the National Bank
Governor
The
Governor
of
the
National
Bank
is
appointed by the Parliament at the proposal of
the President of the Parliament. In accordance
with the Law on the National Bank of Moldova,
the Governor is responsible for outlining the
monetary and foreign exchange initiatives, for
submission to the Executive Board and for
their implementation.
The Governor, also,
organizes and manages the activity of the
bank and its representation in relations with
any legal or physical person both in the
Republic of Moldova and abroad.
Supervisory Board
The
Supervisory
Board
consists
of
seven
members
appointed
by
Parliament
for
a
seven-year
term,
with
the
possibility
of
renewing the mandate. Out of the 7 members,
3 members belong to the executive structure
of the National Bank: the governor, the ﬁrst
deputy-governor and a deputy-governor, and
the other 4 members are not employees of the
NBM. The Chairman of the Supervisory Board
is the Governor of the National Bank.
In exercising its powers attributed by law, the
Supervisory Board (i) approves the annual
report and annual ﬁnancial statements; (ii)
adopts
internal
control
system
standards,
continuously
checks
and
assesses
the
operation of the internal control system; (iii)
adopts the rules of professional ethics; (iv)
determines the remuneration fund of the
National Bank, the level of remuneration of the
members of the Executive Board and the
amount of monthly indemnities of members of
the Supervisory Board who are not members
of the Executive Board;
(v) approves the
National Bank’s cost estimate and investment
allowance and monitors their execution; (vi)
selects, on a tender basis, the external audit
organization; (vii) establishes the method in
which the National Bank Committees are
created and operate; (viii) determines the
nominal value, the design of banknotes and
metallic coins,
procedures of putting into
circulation
and
the
conditions
for
their
withdrawal from circulation.
During the year 2017, the Supervisory Board
held 10 meetings and approved 26 decisions,
on the following issues:
• Approval of the Report on the execution
of
the
cost
estimate,
of
investment
allowances
of
the
National
Bank
of
Moldova and ﬁnancial statements of the
National Bank of Moldova for the year
end of period 31 December 2016;
• Approval
of
the
Regulation
on
the
attributions, competencies and operating
procedures of the Investment Committee
of the National Bank of Moldova;
• Approval of the Report on internal audit
activity in 2016;
• Approval of the Report of the Audit
Committee
of
the
National
Bank
of
Moldova on the monitoring activity in
2016;
• Approval of the annual Report of the
National Bank of Moldova for 2016;
101


• Putting
into
circulation
as
means
of
payment a modernized banknote with the
face value of 5 lei;
• Approval
of
graphic
designs
and
of
nominal values of commemorative and
jubilee coins, issue 2017;
• Approval of modiﬁcation of the nominal
structure of the national currency by
introducing a new nominal value of 2 lei
as metallic coin and replacement of 1, 5
and 10 lei banknotes with new metallic
coins;
• Approval of the cost of estimate and of
allowances for National Bank of Moldova
investments for the year 2018;
• Approval of the Strategic Plan of Internal
Audit of the NBM 2018-2022 and of the
annual internal audit plan for 2018;
• Approval and modiﬁcation of the internal
regulations related to fulﬁlment of its
duties.
In its activity, the Supervisory Board paid
special
attention
to
examination
of
international experience and of best practices,
within
permanent
collaboration
with
representatives of the development partners
of the Republic of Moldova and with other
ﬁnancial-banking institutions on subjects of
common interest.
The Supervisory Board
promoted transparency of decision making
and monitoring of activities of the NBM with
reference to legal attributions.
Executive Board
The
Executive
Board
is
composed
of
5
members appointed by the Parliament: the
governor, the ﬁrst vice-governor and three
vice-governors. The Chairman of the Executive
Board is the Governor of the National Bank.
During 2017, the mandate of one deputy
governor was suspended by law.
According to the Law on the National Bank of
Moldova, the main tasks of the Executive
Board are: (i) establishes the monetary policy
in
the
state;
(ii)
establishes
the
foreign
exchange policy in the state and the exchange
rate regime of the national currency;
(iii)
decides on the procedure of issuing licenses,
authorizations, permits and approvals to be
issued by the National Bank; (iv) examines
results
of
controls
performed
at
entities
supervised by the National Bank and adopts
the related decisions;
(v) decides on the
issuance of claims of the National Bank, the
volume and conditions for their issuance; (vi)
adopts the normative acts of the NBM; (vii)
ensures implementation of decisions of the
Supervisory Board; (viii) plans and organizes
the current activity of the NBM.
During the year 2017, 77 meetings of the
Executive Board were organized, at which 369
decisions were examined and approved.
The most important decisions concerned the
following areas:
• the monetary and foreign exchange policy
of the National Bank of Moldova;
• regulation and banking supervision;
• the supervisory policy of the payments
system in the Republic of Moldova;
• reports and statistics;
• the accounting policies of the National
Bank of Moldova;
• internal regulatory framework.
During the year, 43 decisions of the Executive
Board were issued for publication in the Ofﬁcial
Monitor of the Republic of Moldova.
The
Executive
Board
has
continuously
promoted the application of monetary policy
tools and measures to ensure and maintain
price stability; ensure the economy with the
national
currency
and
credibility
of
the
national currency;
regulate and supervise
ﬁnancial institutions, as well as efﬁciency,
safety, accessibility and innovative character
of payments.
Structure of the governing bodies:
Sergiu CIOCLEA
Governor, Chairman of the Supervisory Board
(SB) and Executive Board (EB)
Vladimir MUNTEANU
First
Deputy
Governor,
SB
and
EB
Vice-President Cristina HAREA
Deputy Governor, member of the SB and of the
EB Ion STURZU
Deputy Governor, member of the EB Aureliu
CINCILEI
Deputy
Governor,
member
of
the
EB
(suspended of right Dumitru URSU
member of the SB Alexandru PELIN
member of the SB Valeriu IAS, AN
member of the SB Vadim ENICOV
member of the SB
102


Committees
In
order
to
ensure
the
efﬁciency
of
the
decision-making process, the Audit Committee
and the Investment Committee operate in the
NBM. These permanent Committees have their
own operating regulations, which detail their
speciﬁc
composition,
attributions
and
responsibilities.
Audit Committee
