The audit committee is made up of 3 members
of the Supervisory Board who are not on the
pay-roll of the National Bank.
The Audit
Committee assists the Supervisory Board by
providing consultancy on: (i) monitoring the
ﬁnancial reporting process of the National
Bank; (ii) monitoring the effectiveness of the
internal control and risk management system;
(iii) monitoring and directing the internal audit
function; (iv) monitoring the independence
and external audit activity.
In order to ensure the activity and fulﬁllment
of its duties, the Audit Committee in 2017 took
a range of practical measures and actions, of
which the most important are:
• monitoring
the
effectiveness
and
adequacy of the internal control and risk
management framework at NBM level;
• monitor compliance issues that may have
a material impact on the NBM ﬁnancial
statements and/or the reputation of the
National Bank;
• the analysis of the implementation of the
Strategic Internal Audit Plan for the period
2013-2017;
• the analysis and coordination of the
Strategic
Internal
Audit
Plan
for
the
period 2018 - 2022 and its presentation
to the Supervisory Board of the NBM for
examination and approval;
• the analysis and coordination of the
Annual Internal Audit Plan for 2018 and
its presentation to the Supervisory Board
of the NBM for examination and approval.
Investment Committee
The Investment Committee of the National
Bank consists of 10 bank employees, including
2 vice-governors, one of whom is the chairman
of the committee.
The Investment Committee is set up to assist
the Executive Board of the National Bank of
Moldova in reviewing the investment policies
and
strategies
for
managing
the
foreign
currency reserves of the state.
Meetings of the Investment Committee are
usually held monthly, and in the case of major
issues, extraordinary meetings are organized.
The Investment Committee met in 2017 12
times,
in ordinary regime.
During each
meeting, the performance of the investment
portfolio for the previous reporting month, the
evolution and forecasts of the domestic foreign
exchange market and international ﬁnancial
markets,
the
ratings
of
authorized
counterparties and the risks of the investment
portfolio were discussed.
At the same time,
new
counterparties
were
examined
and
included in the list of counterparties authorized
to perform transactions.
Also, periodically,
issues related to short and medium term
investment strategy related to the strategic
allocation of assets were discussed.
External and internal control levels
External control
The Law on the National Bank stipulates two
levels of external control - the annual external
audit of the ﬁnancial statements, of accounts
and registers of the NBM undertaken by a
renowned company and with experience in the
ﬁeld of central bank audit and external public
audit on the legality and compliance of cost
estimates and of allowances for investments
made by the Court of Accounts of the Republic
of Moldova.
Internal control
The
NBM
is
in
the
process
of
aligning
operational risk management and internal
control system with international standards
COSO43, ISO 3100044, with best practices in
the
ﬁeld
(International
Operational
Risk
Working Materials and Studies Group, IORWG),
the National Bank is a member of this group).
The
governance
of
operational
risk
management and the internal control system
at the NBM, according to these standards, is
carried out through three lines of defense.
The
appropriate
establishment
of
control
procedures and activities by subdivisions in
43COSO - The Committee of Sponsoring Organizations of
the Treadway Commission.
44ISO
31000
-
Risk
Management
-
Guidelines,
International Organization for Standardization
103


the process of risk management, based on the
processes management system, is the ﬁrst line
of defense of the National Bank against the
factors that may threaten goals achievement.
For this purpose, each structural subdivision of
the NBM identiﬁes risks and applies a set of
control procedures on a daily basis to manage
risks associated with business processes it
operates with and ensures their effectiveness
and efﬁciency.
The second line of defense is to provide the risk
management framework, process organization,
independent coordination and supervision of
the risk control and risk management process.
The functions of the second level of control
include speciﬁc monitoring mechanisms and
processes to exercise adequate control over
ﬁnancial and operational risks. The second line
of defense is represented by the Investment
Committee and ﬁnancial and operational risk
management structures.
At
this
level
of
defense
is
ensured
the
normative framework regarding operations on
the
ﬁnancial
market,
the
procedures
of
conducting and controlling foreign exchange
operations,
planning
and
organizing
NBM
procurement,
ensuring
the
continuity
of
activity and security of information, including
standards,
policies and plans to manage
incidents
that
disrupt
or
threaten
any
operational function within the NBM. The NBM
keeps record of the incidents that take place,
including measures undertaken and personnel
responsible for these measures.
The third line of defense provides independent
assurance and is represented by the internal
audit. Internal audit grants management and
line
managers
an
adequate
reasonable
independent assurance on the adequacy and
effectiveness of governance, risk management
and internal controls, including the method in
which the ﬁrst and second line of defense
meet the risk management objectives.
Framework of Compliance and Ethics
Code of Conduct of the NBM Employee
The National Bank requires that all employees
comply
with
the
highest
standards
of
professional ethics. For that purpose, the Code
of Conduct is established to address personal
and professional ethics conduct of National
Bank
staff,
including
conﬂict-of-interest
behavior,
conﬁdentiality,
security
and
management of information.
Anti-fraud policy
Anti-Fraud Policy aims to increase fraud risk
awareness and management, identify areas of
fraud
at
risk
and
implement
appropriate
control measures at all levels of the NBM,
which will help prevent and detect fraud,
monitor fraud risk and promote consistent
organizational conduct. Anti-fraud policy also
sets
out
the
conditions
for
conducting
investigations
regarding
prevention
and
investigation of suspicions of fraud.
Implementation
of
anti-fraud
policy,
staff
training on fraud risk and its management,
reporting suspected frauds - all these establish
and develop the anti-fraud culture,
which
discourages
potential
perpetrators
and
reinforces the commitment of the National
Bank staff to combat fraud. The responsibility
of the entire staff of the National Bank makes
a major contribution to creating an enabling
environment for raising awareness of risk and
of consequences of fraud.
The governing bodies of the National Bank are
promoting a zero tolerance policy regarding
fraud and corruption.
Measures to combat money laundering
and terrorist ﬁnancing
The National Bank has established internal
rules on prevention and combating money
laundering
and
terrorist
ﬁnancing
in
the
framework of operations performed.
The
purpose of these rules is non-admittance of
involvement of the National Bank in operations
or activities with the risk of money laundering
and terrorist ﬁnancing.
The
norms
are
in
line
with
international
standards and with national legislation on
customer
knowledge
and
transactions
performed through the National Bank and
establishes the requirements for organizing,
implementing and monitoring compliance by
bank’s
subdivisions
of
the
criteria
for
prevention and combating money laundering
and ﬁnancing terrorism.
The internal reporting system supports the
activity of the NBM in combating money
laundering and terrorist acts ﬁnancing by
systematically centralizing all ﬁndings of the
activity carried out in the domain and bringing
them to acknowledgement of the member of
the Executive Board, who coordinates the
activity. For high risk operations, reporting is
done to the Executive Board.
104


Information security policy
The information security policy within the
National Bank ensures security of information
in all its forms and protection of informational
resources managed in accordance with the
bank’s needs and provisions of the legal
framework.
The members of the Supervisory Board and of
the Executive Board, as well as any employee
of the National Bank are obliged to ensure
security of information they possess and to
which they have access. At the same time, the
responsibilities in the process of ensuring
information security, information management,
monitoring of utilization and distribution of
information, reporting of information security
incidents, as well as the method of their
investigation,
are
clearly
established
and
clearly divided.
Public
access
to
normative
acts,
statistics and publications of the NBM
Public access to normative acts, statistics and
publications of the NBM ensures a high degree
of transparency, while respecting the NBM
independence as well as conﬁdentiality of
certain aspects related to the fulﬁllment of the
NBM attributions. Publication of various data
and analysis related to the attributions of the
National
Bank
of
Moldova,
as
well
as
promotion of an open relationship with the
target groups are aspects of major importance
in external communication of the bank.
The National Bank of Moldova pays increased
attention to the reverse connection in the
communication scheme.
In such a way, by
regular and frequent communications, the
National Bank provides detailed explanations
about its activity and activities of the banking
sector.
The functioning of the interactive
database on the NBM website provides public
access
to
information,
internal
data
and
analysis and aims to provide accurate, current,
clear and transparent information for creating
a public opinion regarding the decisions of the
National Bank, but also for conducting various
studies and research.
9.2
The
strategic
plan
of
the
National
Bank
of
Moldova
The strategic plan of the National Bank of
Moldova for the years 2013-2017
The strategic plan aims to strengthen the role
of
the
NBM
in
society
by
fulﬁlling
its
responsibilites, of ensuring efﬁcient use of
resources and reducing exposure to risks. The
priorities set by the objectives of the plan are
designed to ensure a high level of efﬁciency,
transparency and performance, by aligning to
best international practices of communication,
credibility and corporate governance.
The strategic plan of the NBM for the years
2013-2017 includes several key elements such
as mission, vision, values, core business areas,
strategic objectives, etc.
Mission of the National Bank of Moldova
The fundamental objective of the National
Bank of Moldova is to ensure and maintain
price
stability.
Without
prejudice
to
its
fundamental objective, the National Bank of
Moldova promotes and maintains a ﬁnancial
system
based
on
market
principles
and
supports the general economic policy of the
state.
Vision of the National Bank of Moldova
The
National
Bank
of
Moldova
is
an
independent,
efﬁcient and credible public
authority,
which
promotes
an
adequate
monetary policy and contributes to ensuring
the integrity and stability of the ﬁnancial
system, by consistently applying the best
international
practices
for
the
interest
of
society.
Values of the National Bank of Moldova
Civic Commitment – activity aimed at the
beneﬁt of the public interest;
Efﬁciency – application of modern innovations
and technologies in the use of resources;
Excellence – demonstrating competence and
professionalism in the exercise of duties and
adoption of best international practices;
Transparency
–
transparent
and
impartial
105


attitudes in decision-making;
Responsibility – responsible attitude towards
society in the implementation of established
tasks;
Integrity – ethical behavior at the bank and
every employee level;
Credibility
–
conduct
that
inspires
and
maintains public trust.
By
setting
up
the
core
business
areas,
emphasis is placed on the concrete functions
of the NBM, these areas actually reﬂecting the
mission of the NBM and constitute its core
pillars. The principal areas of activity are serve
as a basis for formulating strategic objectives,
as follows:
Monetary policy – the application of monetary
policy tools and measures to ensure and
maintain price stability;
Currency issue – securing the economy with
the
national
currency
and
promoting
the
credibility of the national currency;
Supervision of ﬁnancial institutions – regulate
and supervise the work of ﬁnancial institutions;
Payment system – promoting the efﬁciency,
safety, accessibility and innovative character
of payments. Strategic objectives are those
priorities that the NBM establishes based on its
mission for a determined period in order to
contribute to the realization of its vision.
Strategic objectives of the NBM for the period
2013-2017 are:
• Ensure price stability;
• Increasing the credibility of the national
currency;
• Development of NBM oversight function;
• Promote cashless payments and reduce
cash in circulation;
• Strengthen the ﬁnancial stability function;
• Improve external communication;
• Strengthening the image and credibility of
the NBM;
• Increase
the
efﬁciency
of
NBM’s
operational activity;
• Optimize human resource management;
• Development of corporate governance.
The year 2017 marks the end of the ﬁrst
Strategic Plan of the NBM for the years
2013-2017. During the implementation of the
Strategic Plan, the NBM followed development
directions in strict connection with its mission
and vision, which were oriented towards the
creation of a strong and competitive ﬁnancial
sector.
9.3
Management
and
organization
According to the National Bank of Moldova
Strategic Plan for 2013-2017, out of the ten
strategic objectives, two are related to human
resources: optimization of human resources
management and development of corporate
governance.
Respectively,
the
key
priorities
of
the
departments
dealing
directly
with
human
resources management and development, in
2017, were focused speciﬁcally on these areas;
the measures taken are being detailed in
continuation.
Organizational evolutions
In 2017, a range of modiﬁcations took place
regarding the organizational structure of the
National Bank of Moldova, which meant to
contribute to the achievement of the Bank’s
strategic
objectives
and
of
its
core
competencies,
as well as to increase its
efﬁciency by lowering the hierarchical levels.
At the same time, these changes aim to
increase the capacity of the NBM to adapt to
changes in the economic environment by
redeﬁning
responsibilities,
restructuring
activities,
and
strengthening
institutional
capacity in the long run.
Thus, functions
related to the NBM core competencies have
been
developed
through
the
creation
of
specialized
subdivisions
in
the
areas
of
supervision,
resolution,
ﬁnancial
stability,
combating money laundering and terrorist
ﬁnancing, etc.
At the same time, compared to 2016, the
number of managerial positions of the bank
was reduced by about 4%, with the reduction
of 30 sections.
Contrary the ratio of units
related to the NBM core activities increased by
about 3% compared to the number of units in
the support and governance area.
These
optimizations
had
a
positive
impact
on
efﬁcient utilization of the banks’ resources of
personnel, considering the extension of the
institution’s responsibilities as a central bank.
106


9.4
Internal audit
Internal audit at the NBM is an independent
activity of objective assurance and counselling,
designated to add value and improve the
institution’s activities. Internal audit helps the
institution achieve its objectives through a
systemic and disciplined approach within the
framework of assessment and enhancement of
effectiveness of risk management, control and
governance
processes
by
providing
the
institution’s
governing
bodies
with
a
reasonable and objective assurance on their
functionality.
The structure and positioning of the internal
audit within the NBM is in line with the
requirements of the Law on the National Bank
of Moldova45, the Standard of Qualiﬁcation46
and good practices in the ﬁeld. Accordingly,
the
NBM
Internal
Audit
Department
is
organized and operates in accordance with a
normative and methodological framework in
line with international standards.47.
The
applicable regulatory framework assures the
responsibility of IAD staff to avoid conﬂict of
interest and to ensure objectivity in carrying
out audit missions.
Internal DAI procedures
ensure reporting of cases of damage to the
independence and / or objectivity of IAD/ IAD
employees.
In
the
audit
activity,
IAD
employees are guided by the Audit Manual. In
2017,
the
audit
activity
was
performed
according to the approved audit plan and set
priorities. IAD in its work did not encountered
obstacles in expressing independent opinion.
The IAD Director has regularly reported the
results of the audit work to the NBM governing
bodies:
the
Audit
Committee
and
the
Supervisory Board. The annual internal 2017
audit
plan
was
fulﬁlled
to
a
100%
accomplishment.
The
Internal
Audit
Department fulﬁlled the insurance missions,
audited ﬁnancial statements and the NBM
budget
execution
report,
audited
and
monitored areas of increased-risk.
Audit
missions
and
advisory
activities
have
materialized through: 15 insurance missions,
37 reports, 31 consulting / advisory advice to
managers and members of the governing
bodies of the National Bank.
At
the
same
time,
the
internal
auditors
participated
as
technical-analytical
45Law on the National Bank of Moldova no. 548-XIII of
21.07.1995 - Article 33 (4)
46Standard IIA - 1100 “Independence and Objectivity”)
47International Standards for the Professional Practice of
Internal Auditing
consultants,
carrying
out
monitoring
and
counseling in 5 major projects of the NBM. The
opinion and proposals of the internal auditors
on project areas and risks were submitted,
either as a result of the examination of the
project documentation or at the request of the
project management committee.
In order to monitor the actions taken to
implement the recommendations made in the
special audit reports / special reports, DAI
performs quarterly follow-up monitoring to
assess the extent to which recommendations
were met both in 2017 and in previous years.
Also, in 2017, DAI developed the Strategic
Internal
Audit
Plan
for
2018-2022.
The
planning
process
has
been
performed
according to existing regulations and best
practices and based on the risk assessment
procedure.
For the implementation of the
Strategic Plan, the Annual Internal Audit Plan
2018
was
also
developed.
The
above-mentioned plans were coordinated and
approved.
9.5
Information technology
During
the
year
2017,
in
the
ﬁeld
of
information and communication technology (in
continuation ICT), the most important results
were achieved in the major projects at the
NBM level, but also as a result of certain
projects for strengthening ICT infrastructure
and capacities.
In total, 8 projects were implemented, of which
4 transversal-projects at NBM level, the other
projects being internal at subdivision level.
Referring
to
transversal
projects,
their
description is presented in Table A.32 “The
Evolution of Major Projects of the NBM during
2017”. From the other category, ﬁrst of all, it is
worth
mentioning
the
project
aimed
at
implementing a new remote modular reserve
data center.
The necessity for this project
derives
from
the
premise
that
ensuring
continuity of NBM business processes is a
critical success factor in the realization of the
NBM mission.
As a result,
the solution
implemented for the new Reserve Data Center
corresponds to the highest standards in the
ﬁeld, as well as to the requirements and
demands
for
reliability
and
performance
characteristic
to
central
banks.
Other
important
achievements
pertained,
in
particular, to the extension of infrastructure
107


capabilities,
as
a
proactive
measure
for
continuous
increase
of
ICT
resources
necessities, resulting from the implementation
of new solutions and respectively, from the
increase of the level of automation of activities
at the NBM level.
Overall, all efforts made and achievements
obtained in the ICT ﬁeld, ultimately, pursue
the same common goal of ensuring a proactive
and effective alignment of ICT capabilities with
the strategic and operational needs of the NBM.

Optimizing the payroll system
The ﬁrst stage of implementation of a new
payroll system for employees of the National
Bank
was
accomplished
by
uniformity
of
payment of wages, which has the following
beneﬁts: increasing the transparency of the
system; awareness of individual salary level;
increasing attractiveness of the NBM as an
employer.
The second step, which involves
establishing a direct relationship between the
value of the position and the level of salary, as
well as ensuring internal equity and external
competitiveness of salary, is to be achieved in
2018 as part of an implementation project of
an integrated human resources management
system with the aid of a consulting company
with international experience in the ﬁeld.

In 2017, volunteer staff turnover rate was
4.98%,
which
represented
a
decrease,
compared to 2016, when it registered a level
of 8.43%. Analyzing volunteer staff turnover
rate by hierarchical levels, for managerial
positions in 2017 it reached 5.16%, and for
executive positions 4.91%.

needs
of

Chapter 10
Analysis of the ﬁnancial situation for 2017
10.1
General
considerations
In accordance with the Law on the National
Bank of Moldova no. 548-XIII of 21 July 1995
(republished in the Ofﬁcial Monitor of the
Republic of Moldova, 2015, no.
297-300,
art.554), the fundamental objective of the
National Bank of Moldova is to ensure and
maintain price stability.
The basic tasks of the National Bank are
established in the Law on the National Bank of
Moldova.
In
accordance
with
the
legal
provisions, the operations of the National Bank
of Moldova are performed in order to promote
and
maintain
the
monetary
and
foreign
exchange
policy
in
the
state,
to
ensure
stability of the banking system, issuance of
national currency, management of currency
reserves of the state, provision of payment
services and issuance of electronic money, etc.
Respectively, maximization of proﬁt from the
activity of the National Bank of Moldova is not
an end in itself and cannot serve as an
impediment to the achievement of the core
objective and attributions.
10.2
Available
proﬁt
for
distribution
The ﬁnancial result of the National Bank of
Moldova depends directly on the necessity to
perform
monetary
and
foreign
exchange
operations in order to achieve the fundamental
objective and fulﬁlment of the Bank’s core
tasks.
Evolution
of
available
proﬁt
for
distribution during the period 2015-2017 is
reﬂected in chart 10.1.
On December 31, 2017, the level of statutory
capital
in
the
total
monetary
liabilities
constituted about 5.08% (on December 31,
2016: 6.23%).
Chart
10.1
Evolution
of
available
proﬁt

1960.89

Source: NBM
According to the ﬁnancial statement, on 31
December 2017, the bank recorded a total loss
of MDL (95.31) million, compared to December
31, 2016 when it recorded a total loss of MDL
(262.15) million, this fact being determined, to
a large extent, by the following developments
with signiﬁcant impact:
• Increase of interest income on other state
securities held to maturity - government
bonds of MDL 467.26 million compared to
2016 (MDL 150.73 million) representing
the revenues from state bonds issued on
4 October 2016 for a total amount of MDL
13 341.20 million for the Ministry of
Finance to execute payment obligations
derived
from
state
guarantees.
Government bonds with maturities of 1-9
years were issued with the coupon rate of
1.40%
annually,
and
those
with
maturities of 10-25 years with the coupon
rate of 5.30% per annum.
On the 31 of December 2017, the total
amount of state bonds constituted MDL
13 291.20 million (on 31 December 2016:
MDL 13 341.20 million).
• Decrease of interest expenses on banks’
required reserves by MDL 265.22 million
compared to 2016 (MDL 799.78 million)
following the gradual decrease of interest
rates applied to main monetary policy
operations. The average rate paid on the
required reserves in Moldovan Lei during
the
year
2017
varied
between
a
111


maximum of 6.0% annually and to a
minimum
of
3.56%
annually
(2016:
16.50% - 6%). The amount of required
reserves maintained by banks as of 31
December
2017
amounted
to
MDL
12 638.58 million, USD 70.65 million and
EUR 118.53 million (December 31, 2016:
MDL 10 063.69 million, USD 61.34 million
and EUR 105.54 million). The level of the
required reserves held in Moldovan lei
was gradually increased, during the year
2017, from 35% to 40%, in 2016 being
maintained at 35%. The level of required
reserves in foreign currency, USD and
EUR,
has
not
been
changed
for
six
consecutive years, accounting for 14% of
the calculation base. The calculation base
for the required reserves in Moldovan lei
during
the
year
2017
increased,
registering an increase of about 9.9%
compared to the end of the previous year.
• Decrease
in
revenues
from
foreign
currency
operations
by
MDL
178.21
million compared to 2016 (MDL 238.75
million) as a result of appreciation of the
MDL against the USD by about 14.42%,
versus GBP (by 6.20%) and compared to
EUR by 2.30%, the revenues being mainly
recorded due to the sales of foreign
currency.
Most of the revenues were
achieved
between
1
January
and
5
February 2017, during which the MDL
depreciated compared to EUR by 3.16%
and compared to the GBP by 3.10%.
In
2016,
the
revenues
were
higher,
following
the
depreciation
of
the
exchange rate of MDL versus the USD by
about 1.64%, being registered, mainly,
due to sale of foreign currency.
• Registration
of
revenues
from
the
recovery of certain expenditures related
to liquidation procedures of the banks in
liquidation in the amount of MDL 75.24
million (2016: MDL 0 million).
The impact of events on realizing a total loss,
by ﬁelds of activity performed by the National
Bank of Moldova during 2017 is presented in
table A.24.
Impact
of
implementation
of
the
monetary policy and of activities related
to relations with the Government of the
Republic
of
Moldova
on
the
ﬁnancial
situation
of
the
National
Bank
of
Moldova
During the year 2017, the National Bank of
Moldova continued to utilize ﬁnancial tools to
absorb excess liquidity.
Expenditures from
sterilization
operations
performed
through
issuance of certiﬁcates of the National Bank of
Moldova have increased, amounting to about
MDL 520 million in 2017 compared to about
MDL 346 million in 2016.
The increase of
expense on amortization of the discount of
CBN was determined by the increase in the
average daily balance of certiﬁcates issued by
the NBM, which during the reporting period
amounted to MDL 6 358.07 million (2016: MDL
2 959 million).
At the same time, during the 2017, as a result
of
gradual
decrease
of
the
interest
rate
applicable to required reserve from the funds
attracted in Moldovan lei from 6% to 3.56%
(2016: from 16.50% to 6%), the NBM recorded
smaller net expenditures by about MDL 264
million, compared to 2016.
The interest rates paid on attracted funds in
freely convertible currencies varied from 0.6%
to 0.3% (2016: variation range from 0.2% to
0.65%).
The set of monetary policy tools applied by the
National Bank of Moldova during 2017 and the
activities
related
to
relations
with
the
Government
of
the
Republic
of
Moldova
generated a net loss of MDL 448.42 million,
smaller by MDL 291,021 million compared to
the losses in 2016 (Table A.25).
Impact of management of state currency
reserves
operations
on
the
ﬁnancial
situation
of
the
National
Bank
of
Moldova
The ofﬁcial reserve assets of the state, held by
the NBM, increased as of 31 December 2017
as compared to 31 December 2016 by 27.08%
or by about USD 597.33 million (from USD
2 205.93 million to USD 2 803.26 million),
being
invested
predominantly
in
ﬁnancial
instruments denominated in USD, euros and
pounds.
At the same time, during the year
2017 there was an increase in the average
annual volume of international reserves by
112


25.74% or by USD 487.98 million (from USD
1 934.79 million to USD 2 432.77 million).
The net revenues related to the management
of foreign exchange reserves and other foreign
exchange
operations
in
the
year
2017
constituted MDL 541.52 million, decreasing by
about MDL 124.20 million as compared to the
previous year, when revenues amounted to
MDL 665.72 million.
It should be mentioned that in 2017 there was
a signiﬁcant decrease in net income from
currency rate differentials of MDL 193.81
million (the net income from currency rate
differentials in 2017 amounted to MDL 44.62
million compared to MDL 238.43 million in
2016) due to the appreciation of the MDL
exchange rate against the USD - 14.42%
during the year 2017 (2016: depreciation of
1.64%) and the slowdown of appreciation of
the exchange rate of MDL versus EUR - 2.30%
(2016:
2.74%) and GBP - 6.20 % (2016:
15.88%).
The impact of currency reserves management
operations on the ﬁnancial situation of the
National Bank of Moldova is presented in table
A.26.
The average proﬁtability ratio for managing
currency reserves for 2017 decreased by
0.07 pp. to 1.14% (2016: 1.21%), as a result of
a lower currency exchange rate proﬁt than in
the previous year (Chart 10.2).

It should be noted that, in accordance with
international best practices as well as with the
provisions of the Law on the National Bank of
Moldova, the main criteria for the selection of
reserve assets are the safety of core principal
amounts and its liquidity.
Thus, only after
ensuring these two criteria, the proﬁtability of
instruments in which the foreign exchange
reserves of the state are invested is taken into
account.
The evolution of the ofﬁcial exchange rate of
the MDL against the currencies in which
currency reserves are held had a signiﬁcant
impact
on
the
ﬁnancial
situation
of
the
National Bank of Moldova. Its dynamics and
the average annual values are reﬂected in
Table A.27.
Thus, at the end of 2017, unrealized losses
were recorded as a result of the appreciation
of the MDL against currencies, which are
components of the investment portfolio, i.e.
against USD by 14.42%, against EUR by 2.30%
and against GBP by 6.20%. Due to exchange
rate differentials at revaluation of currency
stocks which amounted to MDL (4 851.88)
million and the decrease in prices of securities
held in foreign currencies on international
markets the NBM incurred unrealized losses
from revaluation of the investment securities
in the amount of about MDL (28.89) million.
According to the Law on the National Bank of
Moldova, the unrealized losses were offset
with
unrealized
revenues
from
the
corresponding reserve accounts.
At the end of 2016, unrealized losses from the
exchange
rate
differentials
from
the
revaluation
of
foreign
exchange
reserves
represented an amount of MDL (1 033.31)
million
and
unrealized
losses
from
the
differentials of the revaluation of investment
securities portfolio an amount of MDL (18.15)
million.
10.3
Capital and reserves
The activities carried out by the National Bank
of Moldova during the year 2017, in order to
fulﬁl its core duties,
are reﬂected in the
structure of the bank’s balance sheet, as
shown in Table A.28.
The capital and reserves situation is presented
in Table A.29.
In accordance with the art. 19 of the Law on
National
Bank
of
Moldova
no.548-XIII
of
21.07.1995, the general reserve fund shall be
used exclusively to cover losses registered
according to the result of the activities at the
end of the ﬁnancial year.
Thus, on 31 December 2017, the general
reserve
fund
was
used
to
cover
losses
113


registered at the end of the ﬁnancial year in
the amount of MDL 95.31 million, the reserve
fund decreasing to MDL 1 448.48 million.
Consequently,
the
statutory
capital
was
reduced, amounting to MDL 2 351.45 million.
By December 31, 2017, the Bank’s capital and
reserves amounted to a total of MDL 4 004.72
million, decreasing as compared to December
31, 2016. This decrease was due to coverage
of
unrealized
losses
from
exchange
differentials as a result of the revaluation of
foreign currency stocks in the amount of MDL
(4
851.88)
million
and
of
coverage
of
unrealized
losses
from
revaluation
of
investment securities in the amount of MDL
(28.89) million.
Other
reserves
include
the
reserve
of
cumulative
revenue
and
losses
from
revaluation
of
government
securities,
classiﬁed as available for sale, and favorable
differentials from revaluation of monetary gold.
The revaluation of these assets represents
changes in global market values that may
subsequently register proﬁts or loss.
By December 31, 2017, the level of statutory
capital in total monetary liabilities amounted to
about 5.08% (by December 31, 2016: 6.23%).
114


Chapter 11
External communication
The National Bank of Moldova continued to
improve its communication by using new tools
and informational channels.
In 2017, the
Communication Strategy was approved. The
Strategy was adapted to new realities and has
the objective to encourage opening of the
National Bank to the public on the principles of
transparency, accessibility, clarity, actuality
and predictability.
The importance of communication of the
National Bank of Moldova derives from its
mission, vision, and its values and relates to
the strategic objectives of the bank.
The
National Bank makes its decisions known to
the public, explains their meaning and the
expected
effect
on
the
economy,
the
ﬁnancial-banking
sector
and
related
participants. In this context, more than 600
comments
to
journalists’
requests
were
provided,
6
press
conferences
including
quarterly reports and 9 thematic interviews
were
organized.
The
researches
and
macroeconomic analysis performed by the
NBM were presented in 27 publications.
The
use
of
new
tools
and
modern
communication channels, such as multimedia
and social networks, as well as openness to
the public, have generated positive results
conﬁrmed by monitoring the implementation
of the Communication Strategy on a quarterly
basis.
As a result, the interest of users increased
regarding monetary policy issues, banking
supervision,
regulatory
acts
developed
together
with
alignment
to
the
latest
international
banking
regulatory
principles
adopted in 2010, known under the generic title
BASEL III. At the same time, the impact of
National Bank’s posting on social networks
increased:
Facebook,
YouTube,
Twitter,
LinkedIn (For example, from 100 to 4 000
views on Facebook), which also reﬂects the
interest of target groups,
which are less
familiar with ﬁnancial-banking issues.
In the context of openness to the public, the
National
Bank
continued
to
pay
special
attention to the ﬁnancial education process,
receiving as guests over 2,000 children and
young people. The range of traditional projects
developed
in
this
respect
included:
International Week of Financial Education,
Open Doors Day, International Savings Day,
etc. was completed with new ones: "A Holiday
Day at NBM" and "From Student to Student"
(attracting
high
school
students
of
the
Academy of Economic Sciences of Moldova to
volunteer in educational activities).
As well, in the context of assuming a social
communication, the National Bank has been
involved in trainings on communication in
situations of crisis and emergencies.
115

Table A.17: Investment limits and constraints applied by the NBM for investment risk management
Risks
Techniques to minimize the investment risk
Credit risk
* Investing in secure, high credit quality counterparts, selected based on the Standard&Poor, Moody and Fitch’s Ratings (the average rating
quoted by the above three agencies is to be used).
* Setting the minimum allowable rating for authorized counterparts’ transactions.
* Establishing investment limits depending on: the type, duration and rating of investment instruments, as well as the foreign exchange
composition.
* Daily monitoring of investment limits and compliance with credit limits.
* Quantiﬁcation of credit risk by using the default coefﬁcients determined by the Standard&Poor’s rating agency, for each type of
rating and term to maturity.
Market risk
* Daily market risk monitoring by assessing at market price the unrealized gains and losses incurred as a result of exchange rate
ﬂuctuations and developments in securities’ prices.
* Managing and monitoring the market risk of the investment securities portfolio by using the value-at-risk methodology (VaR and CVAR), which
estimates potential ﬁnancial losses based on a range of parameters and assumptions determined for different changes in market conditions.
* Monitoring the interest rate risk and determining (by using PV01) the sensitivity of the investment portfolio securities’ prices to interest
rate ﬂuctuations.
* Establishing by the NBM Executive Board of the ofﬁcial currency composition for the investment portfolio, that aims to cover foreign exchange
risk, the portfolio’s optimization through optimal proportioning of foreign exchange assets and liabilities, as well as by setting an investment
horizon to ensure the meeting of current external obligations and implementation of the monetary and foreign exchange policies of the
state. The ofﬁcial currency composition is determined based on an extensive analysis of: the foreign exchange composition of trade
in goods and services, the external debt, the intervention currency, etc. The reserves currency composition may deviate within
+/- 10% from the ofﬁcial currency composition.
* Establishing by the NBM’s Investment Committee of the currency composition of each sub-portfolio, and allowed deviations from it.
* Complying with the ofﬁcial currency composition and currency composition of each sub-portfolio.
Liquidity risk
* Securing liquidity through demand deposits and investing in securities with high liquidity and low risk.
* Ensuring daily liquidity risk monitoring by using market benchmarks set for indexed investment sub-portfolios grouped by instrument type
and foreign currency. The applied benchmarks are market indices that are recognized and used worldwide to assess the performance and
risks of investment portfolios.
* The NBM’s investment policy allows active reserves management to ensure limited instrument and duration deviation of +/- 20%
from the benchmark.
Source: NBM
133

3. Development 
of the NBM’s 
supervisory 
function 
To streamline the 
processes of 
authorisation and 
storage of regulatory 
acts. To strengthen 
the NBM’s capacity in 
the field of increasing 
the level of

of licensed banks 
through an ongoing

Q III, 2015 
In 2017, the public bidding and 
procurement procedure was 
carried out, following which the 
NBM selected the supplier of 
the IT solution for streamlining 
the licensing, authorisation and 
notification processes. By the 
end of the fourth quarter of 
2017, the analysis and design 
phases were finalised, during 
which the following documents 
were developed and submitted: 
the technical solution 
document; the document on the 
configuration / settings of the 
application; and the document 
on the conceptual architecture 
of the solution. In addition, the 
application’s implementation / 
development phase was 
launched. 
 
 
Completion of the application’s 
implementation / development 
phase. User trainings and start 
of the application’s testing 
phase. Preparation for system 
deployment and, later, for a 
pilot operation of the system. 
Final acceptance and 
completion of the project.

Q III,  2014 
In the second quarter of 2017, 
the NBM’s Executive Board 
decided to merge the project on 
the implementation of the IT 
solution for streamlining the 
shareholder transparency 
monitoring process with the 
project on the remote analysis 
in the field of the prevention 
and combating of money 
laundering and terrorist 
financing. Following the 
merger, a new set of tender 
documents was developed. In 
this context, the NBM organised 
presentation sessions with the 
participation of the IT solution 
suppliers, recognised on the 
local market. The NBM 
identified the optimal 
procurement procedure, 
intending to initiate the 
procurement procedure. 
To initiate and conduct the 
procurement and 
implementation of the IT 
solution.

3. Strengthening 
the NBM’s 
supervisory 
function 
Implementation of 
the provisions of the 
Basel III Agreement to 
provide a more 
flexible framework of 
prudential 
regulations that 
would ensure setting 
of capital adequacy 
requirements that are 
appropriate to the 
risk profile of 
banking institutions. 
Enhancing the 
stability of the 
banking system and 
fostering 
transparency and 
order in the market.  
 
June 2013 
The NBM approved the Law on 
the Banking Activity, based on 
which the regulatory and

Q I,  2017 
The NBM conducted a public 
tendering procedure, following 
which the contract was signed 
with the award-winning service 
supplier. In the third quarter of 
2017, the implementation of the 
new integrated human 
resources management system 
was launched. The first two 
stages of the project were 
completed. The first stage 
involved redefining functions, 
the evaluation of job position, 
defining job families; analytical 
evaluation of all job positions 
within the NBM; training the 
NBM staff and transferring the 
know-how on the methodology 
of job position evaluation; the 
elaboration of the matrix of 
NBM’s job positions; 
determining the number of job 
families. During the second 
stage, a draft document of the 
new salary policy for the NBM’s 
personnel was elaborated. 
 
Analysis and elaboration of a 
career development system. 
Analysis and elaboration of the 
managerial skills development 
system. 
Defining the talent 
management program. 
Final acceptance and 
completion of the project.
